The log loses reports without a trace when the host is saturated (none of 3,000 at a quiet 440 a second, one to five of 1,600 on a saturated host, 7 to 20 percent of a burst of 40,000 a second), so an empty `observedMounts` from a macOS run used to mean only that no report arrived. The audit of each trial now reads a file beneath its own directory, one no target may open, through the sandbox's own token every 50 ms and once more when the trial ends, and counts the canary reads the log delivered against the ones sent (a canary counts as sent when it is attempted, so one a target killed, one the host could not start and a stretch of more than 200 ms in which a target froze the runtime all count as undelivered and leave the trial lossy). `run.json`'s `observedMountsChannel` lists each audited trial with its `conditionArm`, `trialIndex`, `canariesSent`, `canariesDelivered`, `logReportedLoss` and `completeness` (`lossy` when the log lost a canary or reported lost events, `complete` otherwise), and the summary line of `run` names every lossy trial with the canaries lost out of the canaries sent. A canary samples the log every 50 ms, so a single report can still drop between two canaries on a saturated host. A Linux trial records `complete` with no canary sent, since `strace` reports every traced syscall of the call. The reference's `### File-system confinement` states the measurements and the field, `test:evaluate-confinement` runs a `log` that drops every second report through the real CLI (the test-only `TEA_EVALUATE_AUDIT_LOG` names it), and the gaps guide tells a reader to check a trial's entry before trusting an empty list.
- Command evaluators can declare `installState.source` as `tree` or `lockfile` for an installed framework (Story 1.73). The observed digest enters run artifacts and scoring configuration, so a package patch or lockfile-entry change under the same version changes scoring. Probe failures and mid-run changes exit 12 before an affected trial seals. The shipped Node probe and evaluator guide cover both sources; tree hashes retain file execute status while ignoring non-executable permission differences across installs.
- The pantry evaluation now qualifies a copied-target malformed-request guard mutation in development and held-out probes (Story 1.56). Autoevals and eval-quality score three caught trials per defect probe while the malformed clean control passes three trials.
- Sealed-brief evaluator runs now observe the installed agent CLI version through its adapter (Story 1.72, AD-21). The version enters `tea.evaluatorAgentVersion` and `run.json`, so an upgrade changes the configuration digest and scoring version. A bounded probe runs before calibration or qualification; rechecks around each agent launch and after each trial stop changed or unreadable versions with exit 12 before an affected vote or trial set is sealed. A standalone argument delimiter is refused, the trial ceiling includes supervisor cleanup and bounded Windows Job Object setup, and sealed resource use includes the post-attempt and post-trial reads. A custom command names its own version in a keyed JSON response (Story 1.76).
- A command evaluator's `evaluator/frameworks.json` may set `probe.probeTimeoutMs` from 1 to 60,000 ms for each installed framework (Story 1.71, AD-21). An omitted value defaults to 10,000 ms. Each version probe runs for the smaller of its declared bound and `evaluator.timeoutMs`, including the initial read, trial rechecks and calibration. `framework-versions.json` records the effective bound; historical version 1 artifacts without it remain valid. A timed-out probe exits 12 as an unreadable dependency, skips later declared probes and seals no affected trial record. Its diagnostic names the evaluator cap when it applies. The trial resource ceiling uses the same bound, and the Evaluate starters, evaluator guide and public CLI reference give the total cost across initial, trial and calibration reads.
- Evaluate API probes can send canonical base64 raw request bytes with a declared content type through the published eval-quality engine. Scored interaction steps and defect selectors carry the exact raw body, with `callInputs.bodyEncoding` distinguishing raw bytes from a JSON object with the same keys. The AI feature fixture scores a malformed JSON parser mutation over three trials. The HTTP port preserves bytes, enforces request caps and redirects, and frames an empty raw body distinctly from an absent body.
- Evaluate's guides teach file-system confinement (Story 1.61, AD-8, AD-16, AD-18). `references/harness.md` gains `## Declare what a confined target reads`: the registry entry's `systemPaths`, what the audit lists as `observedMounts` and why `score` exits 3 for each, and a tagged `evaluation.json` fragment that `test:evaluate-guidance` validates against the runtime schema. `references/run.md` gains `## Run confined`: Seatbelt on macOS and Bubblewrap on Linux with the observer each needs, the exit 12 refusal of a host with no mechanism, the `"confinement": false` opt-out, the network namespace and the `"network": "host"` declaration, and the `confinement` and `hostNetwork` fields of `run.json`. `references/gaps.md` gains `## Map an isolation violation to its repair`, which maps each kind of observed mount to the repair that closes it. The test marks every passage, so deleting one fails it. The CI guide's `ci-registry` example and live-check note for a Linux skill target move to Story 1.84, since editing `references/ci.md` needs both live capture sessions rerun.
- Evaluate's last stage inspects the adopter's repository and writes its CI plan (Story 2.4, AD-10, AD-11, AD-20). `references/ci.md` teaches four inspections, each under its own heading with a worked example: existing CI, merge flow, release flow and risk profile. It starts every check at AD-10's default tier and records the file or answer behind each placement in `placement.reason`. It lists every deviation for the adopter, keeps the gameability arm, contract-source freshness and oracle-versus-scorer agreement on `pr`, and puts the held-out partition and judge calibration on `scheduled` and `release`. Skill and agent targets run live checks on `scheduled`, `release` and manual dispatch only, with the runner's credential keys declared as `permittedEnvironmentKeys` through the registry's `environmentKeys`. `eval-quality-gates` is offered as opt-in: a section is added only for an adopted gate, an existing section is never rewritten, and each adopted gate joins the plan as a `gate` check on `pr`. The stage accepts the first baseline with the adopter's confirmation, runs every tier that can run on the machine and shows each exit (a blocking exit returns to the stage that owns it), and invokes `bmad-testarch-ci` in edit mode (create mode when the repository has no pipeline file), naming each tier's event.
  The skill ships `assets/evaluation-ci-plan.template.json`, every check at its default tier with an empty `reason`, and `SKILL.md` runs Stage 12. `bmad-testarch-ci` and its how-to now say that Evaluate's CI stage invokes the workflow in edit mode, and its step 03b reads the `release` event from the repository's release or deploy workflow (a tag push, a published release or the deploy workflow's own trigger) and the `merge` event from a `merge_group` the pipeline already lists, else the push to the default branch.
  `test:evaluate-guidance` marks the guide sentence by sentence (each heading, the four worked examples, the placement, tier-run and hand-off rules, and the phrases that would reverse them), holds the guide to the runtime's `DEFAULT_TIERS` by pointer (the template carries the defaults as data), and validates the template and every tagged plan example through the runtime's schema and placement rules. Two fixture repositories under `test/fixtures/evaluate-ci-repos/` carry the AI-feature evaluation: one releases on tags with no model secret in CI, and one deploys nightly with a model secret on scheduled runs. A maintainer session through the local Claude Code CLI ran the stage on each, a `capture-record.json` beside each repository holds the model, the turns, the prompt and the digests of the guide, `SKILL.md`, template and repository files the session read and of the files it wrote, and `test:evaluate-ci` validates both captured plans, asserts that every check the guide keeps is placed, that a live check is placed differently and that every differing `reason` cites a pipeline, merge-rule or release file of its own repository.
- `tea-evaluate digest --evaluation <folder> --calibration-inputs` prints the values a `records` harness copies into `<records>/calibration-judgments.json` (Story 1.67, AD-21, AD-22). It is read-only: it writes nothing under the folder, `corpus-index.json` included, and prints one JSON document with `calibrationDigest` (the value for `tea.judgeCalibrationDigest`), `scorerConfigurationDigest` (over `<records>/evaluator-configuration.json` without its two `tea.judgeCalibration*` bindings, so the harness writes the configuration first and binds afterwards) and `items`, each `{ rubricId, criterionId, scorerInput }` in the labelled file's order, with no `answer` and no `expectedLevel`.
  `check` and `run` derive the same values with the same functions, so a changed derivation changes the output and the verification together. The command exits 10 with a `judge-calibration` finding for an evaluator that is not `records`, a contract with no rubric, an unusable labelled file, or a configuration that is absent, a link, not JSON or off eval-quality's schema.
  The reference and the evaluator guide tell the harness to copy the values verbatim and add only `answer`.
  Bind both calibration values before you seal any record or isolation manifest.
- `bmad-testarch-ci` renders evaluation CI plans into the pipeline (Story 2.3, AD-11, AD-12, AD-20). A new step, `steps-c/step-03b-render-evaluation-plans.md`, detects `ci/evaluation-ci-plan.json` files in create mode, on resume and in edit mode, validates each plan through `tea-evaluate check`, and writes a job per tier the plan places a check on: an install of the evaluations folder with `--prefix`, one `npm exec --prefix <folder> -- tea-evaluate ci --tier <tier>` step named for the tier's checks (a `merge` job runs the `pr` step first), and an upload of the evaluation folder's `runs/` with `if: always()`. The step points at the runtime's schema and `ci-plan.js` and restates no tier table. Generated jobs carry a `# tea-evaluation-plan:` marker with the plan's path, so a later run replaces them. The GitHub Actions template gains the evaluation block. `npm run test:evaluate-ci-render` holds reachability from both entry points, the block parsed as YAML and the plan the fixture adopter carries. The `ci` behavioral suite gains an `evaluation-plan` project with a real capture of the live `eval:ci` run.
- `tea-evaluate` audits the file access of every confined process, whatever its language or environment (Story 1.60, AD-7). A shell script, a Python program, a native binary and a Node process started with an empty environment now list the paths they open outside their workspace, their temp and home directories, the Node installation, the operating system's own directories and the `systemPaths` of their registry entry as the isolation manifest's `observedMounts`, so `score` exits 3 with eval-quality's `mount outside allowlist` reason; a write the mechanism refuses and every access to a withheld path are listed too. The audit is the mechanism's own and no code runs in the target: Seatbelt reports each read its profile allows outside the grants and tags each refusal with a token of the sandbox (git's own index lock excepted), and a `/usr/bin/log stream` child the runtime owns writes the kernel's reports of that token to a file; Bubblewrap runs under `strace -f --seccomp-bpf --decode-pids=pidns`, started outside the namespace, whose trace the runtime reads when the call ends. Both files sit beneath the run's private parent, which every target withholds, so a target can no longer rewrite, truncate or flood the audit (the Node preload and its report file are gone, and `confinement-guard.cjs` with them). On macOS the kernel's reports are lossy (none lost in 3,000 at a quiet host's pace, one to five in 1,600 on a saturated host, 7 to 20 percent of a burst of 40,000 a second), so an empty list from macOS means that no report arrived; no run records the loss yet (Story 1.81 adds a per-trial count to `run.json`), and Linux's trace holds every traced syscall. A host whose observer cannot confirm itself exits 12 naming the cause and the `"confinement": false` opt-out (macOS: the unified log did not report a read the sandbox allowed within five seconds; Linux: no `strace`, or ptrace refused), and an observer that fails during a trial leaves it with no record, so an empty `observedMounts` is never what a broken observer returns. The Linux workflows install `strace` beside `bubblewrap`. A target that runs a toolchain outside the system's directories (a Homebrew `git`, an interpreter under `/opt`) now lists the binary and what it reads unless the registry entry declares its directory in `systemPaths`; the execution of an ungranted binary is a read of it.
- `tea-evaluate` binds a `command` evaluator's run to the framework versions it declares (Story 1.44, AD-21). The evaluator lists each installed framework in the tracked `evaluator/frameworks.json` (`schemaVersion` 1; per framework its package, the one exact version expected and a version probe, an executable under `evaluator/` that prints `{ "package", "version" }`; an empty list for an evaluator with no dependency), so the evaluator tree digest covers it.
  `run` launches each probe as it launches the evaluator and reads the installed versions before the first trial, before each launch of the evaluator and after each trial, calibration included. A package that is missing, installed at another version than declared or changed during the run exits 12 and seals no record for the affected trial.
  The run writes `framework-versions.json` (declared and observed versions, with a failed probe's output) and records the observed versions in `decodingParameters["tea.evaluatorFrameworks"]` and `run.json`'s `evaluator`, so a package upgrade changes the configuration digest and the scoring version once `frameworks.json` and `LEARNED.md` are updated to it. `score` reads the recorded configuration, so a recorded run scores the same after the package moves.
  `check` exits 10 on an absent or malformed declaration, on a probe that is not a tracked executable of the layer, and on an `evaluator/LEARNED.md` whose backticked `package@version` records (in its `## Framework and installed version` section) differ from the declaration, omit a declared package or name an undeclared one.
  The skill ships the probe `assets/evaluators/installed-version.mjs`, an empty declaration, and AgentEvals and promptfoo declaration templates, and the evaluator guide teaches the step for an unfamiliar framework under `## Declare the installed framework versions`.
- `tea-evaluate ci --evaluation <folder> --tier <pr|merge|scheduled|release>` runs the checks an evaluation's CI plan places on one tier (Story 2.2, AD-10, AD-11, AD-12). `ci/evaluation-ci-plan.json` meets a runtime-owned schema and is the only definition of tier membership: each check names its tier, trigger, kind (`evaluate`, run by a closed set of ids, or `gate`, an adopted `eval-quality-gates` command run with no shell), command, enforcement and evidence paths, and records its placement (the chosen tier, AD-10's default and the reason). `ci` and `check` refuse a tier moved off its default with no reason, a deterministic check that needs no secret placed off `pr`, a live check on `pr`, a command not led by its tool, an `enforcement` of `warn` where AD-10 gives no warn (the action always comes from AD-10's table) and a `ci/` directory that is a link (exit 10); a missing plan, an unknown `--tier` and a check that needs an absent `baseline/` exit 64. Every check of the tier runs in plan order, every stage exit passes through verbatim, `ci` passes no `--strict`, CONCERNS is read from the evidence artifact as a warning, and the final exit is the most severe blocking one (64, 12, 5, 4, 3, 13, 11, 10, 2, 1). Each check's exit code, stdout and stderr land byte for byte under `runs/<invocationId>/checks/<id>/`, with `ci.json` beside them. A `gate` check runs in a process group of its own with a `timeoutMs` (600000 by default) and a 64 MiB output bound, exits 12 past either with what it printed kept, and a SIGINT or SIGTERM to `ci` ends the group before `ci` ends. Exactly 64 MiB passes, and a descendant a gate leaves in its group is killed when the gate exits. The replay's scratch directory sits beneath the run's private parent in the user's private root, which a signal removes with everything below it. The `pr` tier replays the committed baseline: it places the baseline's bytes at `runs/<acceptedRun>/` in a scratch copy of the evaluation folder, re-runs eval-quality's preflight and `score` over it and compares the produced verdict, evidence artifacts and strength aggregate with the baseline's byte for byte, exit 13 (evaluation evidence drift, a new exit) when they differ; it also scores the gameability arm with no target launch, reads the engine's `corroboration` on each baseline oracle outcome (exit 11 on `disagrees`, or on `not-evaluable` or `unreached` for an oracle a behavior requires), and warns on a stale baseline (exit 11 on `release`): the current contract, corpus, policy or strength floors differ from the ones the baseline recorded, once per tier whenever `baseline/` exists. The replay scores under the baseline's own floors, so an edit to the floors since the accept is a stale baseline, and a `baseline/scores/` that holds anything besides the accepted score invocation exits 10. The live tiers drive a live preflight, a twin run (at the scoring policy's `minimumTrialCount`), the held-out partition, judge calibration and a strength comparison through `compare`, with the strength floor warning on `scheduled` and blocking on `release`. `npm run test:evaluate-ci` joins the default test chain, the README's count reads 105, and the fixtures `test/fixtures/evaluate/mutation/evals/verdict-ci`, `evaluate-mcp` and `evaluate-api` commit a plan and a baseline each, recorded by real `compare --accept` runs (they carry the recording machine's paths until a later release re-accepts them).
- A confined `tea-evaluate` target keeps the state its agent CLI writes (Story 1.59). Each confined sandbox gets one private home directory, which the runtime makes beneath the run's private parent (`tea-evaluate-target-home-<random>`) and removes with the run (a `run` trial removes its own when the trial ends), and which a read-only directory an agent left in it cannot keep; removing it opens write bits up without following a link, so a link an agent put there cannot lead the runtime into another directory. Seatbelt also answers metadata requests for the root and the parent, so a module load, `realpath`, `mkdir -p` and `cd` inside the home work, while their listings stay refused. The sandbox withholds the user's private root as before and re-grants that one home beneath it: Seatbelt allows it after the root's denial, Bubblewrap binds it into the root's empty file system before the read-only remount, and the audit excepts it from the root's withholding. The target therefore reads and writes its own home and no other (no other trial's, stage's or run's home, and no listing of the parent or the root; Bubblewrap's empty file system over the root shows only the path to the home). `HOME` names the home for every confined call, and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` name `.config`, `.cache` and `.local/share` inside it, over any host value, including one a registry entry's `environmentKeys` names, and for an entry that passes none. The state lasts across the calls of one trial or arm; each independent arm or leg starts with an empty home, a new directory that replaces and removes the old one so a process an earlier arm left running cannot reach it (the next trial, the baseline, mutated and re-pass arms of a qualification, each leg of a `preflight`). Your real home and the project stay unwritable, the evaluation folder and the private root stay closed, a login an agent stored under your real home is not found under the private home (give the agent its API key variable through `environmentKeys`), `targetSandbox` refuses a home inside the workspace or the evaluation folder, and a run with `"confinement": false` keeps the host environment and makes no home. A live skill evaluation no longer has to opt out of confinement for the agent's first state write. There is no registry field for writable paths. The reference's `### File-system confinement` states the home, the Evaluate skill's harness guide shows the confined skill target in a tagged registry example that `test:evaluate-guidance` validates, and `test:evaluate-preflight` and `test:evaluate-confinement` run the stub agent that writes its state under `HOME` with a revert check for each grant.
- `tea-evaluate compare` compares a scored run with the committed baseline and accepts one (Story 2.1, AD-12). Without `--accept` it compares the run's per-probe evidence artifacts with `baseline/` through eval-quality's `compareDominance` and reports `compared` (one engine relation per probe), `first-run` (no baseline) or `refused`, every outcome exit 0; it refuses when the partitions differ, the probe sets differ, a probe's `comparabilityKey` differs (read from the evidence artifacts) or `evalQualityVersion` differs (read from `run.json`), and a version refusal routes to `compare --accept`. With `--accept` it replaces `baseline/` wholesale with a byte-identical snapshot of the run: every input a replay through `score` reads (the contract, sealed brief, scoring policy, evaluator configuration, preflight verdict, qualified probes, observations, sealed records, isolation manifests and the actions artifacts they reference, and the latest score invocation's evidence), its qualification evidence under `baseline/qualification/` as public references `check` verifies, and `baseline.json` with the digests. A run whose `run.json` says `dirty: true` is refused with exit 10 and nothing written, an unscored run exits 64, a partly scored run, a link among the members (a linked directory above one included) or a run `score` would no longer accept (a finding in `score`'s input checks, or a latest `score.json` that records an infrastructure failure or a refused strength aggregate) exits 10, and an accept that fails while staging leaves the old `baseline/` untouched (exit 12). Staging happens under the gitignored `runs/.compare-staging/`, so an interrupted accept never leaves an untracked entry in the committed folder that makes the next `run` record `dirty: true`, and the next `compare --accept` puts a retired baseline back and deletes stale staging. `compare` without `--accept` reads and deletes nothing; with `baseline/` absent and a retired copy present it exits 10 naming the copy. `compare --accept` holds an exclusive lock (one `mkdir` of `runs/.compare-staging.lock`) through recovery, staging, the swap and cleanup, so a second accept exits 12 while it runs; a lock a killed accept left is deleted by hand, as the message says, and Ctrl-C or SIGTERM removes it. A replay through `score` places the baseline's bytes at `runs/<acceptedRun>/` in a scratch copy of the folder, since the sealed records name their artifacts under that path. The comparison logic of `test/lib/compare-dominance.js` and `test/lib/compare-eval-runs.js` moved into `cli/lib/evaluate/compare.js`, and `test:evaluate-compare` joins the `npm test` chain.
- `tea-evaluate score` aggregates the strength of a run through eval-quality (Story 1.45). After the probe loop it calls `eval-quality aggregate-strength` once over the evidence artifacts it copied, the floors in `evaluation.json.strengthFloor` and the run's scoring policy, and copies the engine's `StrengthAggregate` to `scores/<scoreInvocationId>/strength-aggregate.json` beside a `strength-floors.json` copy of the floors and the call's own `aggregate-strength.json` record. Per class the aggregate states the eligible, exercised and caught counts, the rate, whether the class is comparable and the floor decision with its basis; a class with no eligible probe is `null`, an admitted class with no exercised probe has `rate: null`, a trial set below `minimumTrialCount` is not comparable, and clean controls and canaries stay outside every denominator. TeA computes no rate or decision. The copy is held to the digests of the evidence files `score` persisted, to the scored probe set, to the engine version `run.json` recorded and to the floors `evaluation.json` declares (the floors copy is read back after the call, so a replay reads the floors the call read); a disagreement copies nothing and exits 12, a file the held writer refuses mid-step is recorded as `failed` with the call and floors written so far and exits 12, and a floor decision never moves an exit code. When a probe has no evidence artifact the aggregate is not called and `score.json` records it absent with the reason. `score.json`, `partitions.json` and `interpretation.json` carry a `strengthAggregate` pointer, and rerunning the recorded `aggregate-strength` argv with a fresh `--out` reproduces the file byte for byte. Evaluate's `references/run.md` and `references/gaps.md` read the aggregate to hold the adopter's class floors before held-out execution. `strengthFloor` no longer admits `canary`, which the engine keeps outside every class.
- `tea-evaluate` calibrates the rubric scores a `records` evaluator imports (Story 1.40). `check` accepts a `records` evaluation whose contract declares a rubric once its calibration judgments verify. Your harness runs its own scorer over the labelled items in `policy/judge-calibration.json`, handing it each response and no label, and writes the label-free judgments beside its records as `<records>/calibration-judgments.json`. The runtime verifies that every item's `scorerInput` is exactly the label-free observation it derives from the labelled item, that the judgments name the scorer configuration the imported `EvaluatorConfiguration` describes (`scorerConfigurationDigest`), and that the configuration binds `decodingParameters["tea.judgeCalibrationDigest"]` to the labelled file's digest and `decodingParameters["tea.judgeCalibrationMinimumAgreement"]` to `judgeCalibration.minimumAgreement`, so a changed item or minimum changes the configuration digest and the scoring version. `check` and `run` verify the judgments and bindings with one function: absent or unverifiable ones exit 10 (`check` reports the `judge-calibration` rule) before any record is read or copied. `run` then computes exact agreement and the largest level distance per criterion into `runs/<invocationId>/judge-calibration.json` and exits 11 below the minimum with no record copied; `check` does not compute agreement. The runtime cannot prove that the harness ran its scorer; a harness that needs that proof runs its scorer through a `command` evaluator. A `records` evaluation with no rubric imports as before and never reads a judgments file. The reference and the Evaluate skill's evaluator guide describe the file.
- `tea-evaluate` tells a captured value too large to launch from a target that cannot run (Story 1.39). A command step whose `captured` binding makes its launch exceed the system's argument and environment limit is not issued: eval-quality's command-line adapter reports the refusal as a `port-failure` fault whose `portFailureReason` is `launch-too-large`, and the arm records the step in `steps` as `captured-value-unsendable` naming each captured binding of its argument, option and environment channels and that reason (a `stdin` value is written after the launch and is never named), with no observation, no isolation-manifest call and no call record, so the run exits 0 and the probe's outcome is judged on what the target printed. A step with no captured binding in those channels that the system refuses for its size, and a launch failure with no such reason, still stop the run with exit 12, and TeA computes no size of its own. The reference's `### Steps not issued` names the size limit among the values a request cannot carry. `test:evaluate-workflow` runs the workflow fixture with a 2 MiB identifier in the mutated trials, the arm units for each fault shape, the real command-line adapter's refusal of a 2 MiB argument, and the reference reading. A run's fault record carries the `portFailureReason` beside the code. The optional peer's floor moves to `>=4.6.0`, the first release whose command-line adapter carries `portFailureReason`.
- `tea-evaluate run` qualifies a sealed-brief agent evaluator before its verdicts count (Story 1.34). `evaluation.json` gains `evaluatorQualification` (`attempts` of at least 2 and `minimumAgreement` from 0 to 1, both set by the adopter). Before the first trial, `run` runs the agent `attempts` times on the clean arm and on each mutated arm, scores each attempt with `eval-quality score` and writes `evaluator-qualification.json` with each attempt's outcome copied from its evidence artifact. An arm whose lowest probe agreement falls below `minimumAgreement` exits 11 with no trial record; an attempt eval-quality reads as Invalid has no artifact, is recorded with the engine's exit code and `invalid:` lines, and counts as disagreeing. Attempts write nothing under `trials/`, and the two numbers join `EvaluatorConfiguration.decodingParameters`. `tea-evaluate check` exits 10 under `evaluator` when a sealed-brief agent declares no `evaluatorQualification` and when another kind declares one, so an existing sealed-brief-agent evaluation adds the block before `check` passes. The reference's exit 11 row now also lists judge calibration. `test:evaluate-evaluators` runs a stub agent that omits standard input on one attempt of two (agreement 0.5 against 0.9) and compares each outcome with its evidence artifact byte for byte, and `test:evaluate-check` holds the rule.
- The `tea-evaluate` reference lists every denial reason in its own `### Denial reasons` section (Story 1.33). A denied call carries eval-quality's `reason` beside the `forbidden-target` code and detail for a command, a tool call and an HTTP request, in a leg's `faults/` file, a qualification's or trial's fault and a sealed-brief agent's bridge calls. The section is a table of the eleven reasons the target policies decide, from `interface-not-authorized` to `method-not-authorized`, and `test:evaluate-evaluators` holds it equal to the reasons eval-quality exports, so a reason a later release decides fails the case until the reference names it. A case also asserts the reason on a denied command trial step.
- `tea-evaluate preflight` and `run` confine every process they start (Story 1.31). Each target, and every process it starts, a leftover one included, runs under macOS Seatbelt (`sandbox-exec`) or Linux Bubblewrap (`bwrap`): it writes only its workspace and can neither read nor write the evaluation folder, so a target cannot read the contract or rewrite the evidence in `runs/`. Evaluator, agent, judge and HTTP port processes run with the evaluation folder read-only, which closes the window between the layer's re-read under `evaluator/` and the evaluator's launch. Each trial audits the paths its Node processes open outside what it was granted, lists them as the isolation manifest's `observedMounts` so eval-quality records the isolation violation, and grants a registry entry's new `systemPaths`. A host with neither mechanism exits 12 unless `evaluation.json` sets `"confinement": false`; `run.json` records `seatbelt`, `bubblewrap` or `opt-out`, and each forbidden input's note names the confinement that withheld it. Each confined call gets a private temp directory (`TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`), `check` refuses two registry entries that start one target with different `systemPaths`, and `score` says when a run opted out. A missing agent command started through an isolation wrapper now reports `AGENT_NOT_FOUND` naming the agent. Under Bubblewrap each confined process has a process-id namespace and procfs of its own, ends with its parent, and sees no `/run/user`; the target may write only the audit's report file and its own call's status file, and a report cut, padded past the read limit, replaced or removed is listed as an observed mount, checked as each confined call ends. A Bubblewrap that fails before it starts the target ends the call as an infrastructure error and no longer reads as the target's exit code.
- Evaluate runs now send principal and matcher bindings. Principal mappings source credentials from authorized host environment keys and persist opaque labels. `any` matchers use deterministic schema-derived values with a recorded seed (Story 1.30).
- Live `tea-evaluate run` trials record validated CLI target token and cost reports in sealed records and isolation manifests, and identify unreported use in `run.json` (Story 1.29).
- Evaluate now proves its learn-on-the-go procedure with a deterministic Autoevals `ExactMatch` fixture. Its record shows primary-source reads and pass/fail execution before mapping, and `test:evaluate-learned-framework` reruns clean and mutated arms through eval-quality (Story 1.26).
- Evaluate's gap stage now has a blind seeded-weakness proof: a loosened review oracle and a missing type-violating input are diagnosed from engine evidence, repaired, and replayed through `test:evaluate-gap-loop` across development and held-out partitions (Story 1.25).
- Evaluate now has recorded, strong authoring proofs for an HTTP AI feature and a CLI test-review command. Their frozen target inputs, authored suites, controlled mutations, development and held-out evidence, and deterministic replay run in `test:evaluate-authoring` (Story 1.24).
- Evaluate authored and ran the behavioral suite for its own skill, `bmad-testarch-evaluate`, live through the local Claude Code CLI (Story 1.16, AD-15). The committed evaluation at `test/evaluations/bmad-testarch-evaluate/` checks that Stage 11 names the AD-10 class of a failed `tea-evaluate` exit and that Stage 1 maps a web application to `api`, seeds a class swap in the gap guide's exit table with a held-out second swap, and holds each reply to one JSON object through the runner's `--agent-arg`. It replaces the manifest's last `deferred` entry as an `evaluate-authored` suite, and the proof run, its rollback digests and an independent `eval-quality score` replay are recorded in `epic-1-proof.md`. `.prettierignore` keeps the evaluation folder's digested bytes out of formatting.
- Evaluate-authored suites can be registered alongside generator-owned suites, with manifest and evaluation policy checks before they enter the test inventory. `eval:all` records their IDs when it skips them (Story 1.15).
- Evaluate now guides mutation planning, adopter-set risk policy, the private run and score sequence, and evidence-led gap repair through Stage 11. The guidance gate validates tagged mutation files against the runtime schema and checks installed outcome, discipline, preflight and exit mappings. Run-wide class strength is tracked separately because the current engine emits per-probe evidence (Story 1.14).
- Evaluate now teaches how to choose and build an evaluation layer with a six-option rubric, a primary-source procedure for unfamiliar frameworks, and command, AgentEvals, promptfoo, mapping and learning templates. The starters bind promptfoo assertions to stable keys, require tracked AgentEvals references, and reject ambiguous observation selection. The guidance gate runs both framework templates through clean-control and seeded-defect fixture evaluations (Story 1.23).
- Evaluate now guides contract authoring, oracle and rubric design, and adapter wiring for every target kind. A fillable contract skeleton and engine-backed guidance checks cover exact requirements-byte lineage, compile and seal, worked examples, and nonzero validation exits (Story 1.13).
- Evaluate now guides target inspection, adopter-confirmed requirements and corpus design for six target kinds. The evaluation manifest records the confirmed `requirements.md` path and its byte digest, which `tea-evaluate check` validates (Story 1.12).
- `tea-evaluate check` validates an operation's `process` or `outcome` phase against the compiled contract. Scoring writes `interpretation.json` with finding citations, oracle evidence pointers, phase partitions and the first material error, alongside judgment fields copied from eval-quality's evidence artifacts (Story 1.22).
- `tea-evaluate run --partition` selects development or held-out probes. Scoring writes evidence-derived `partitions.json` and a `gap-view.json` that redacts held-out probe details. Rubric runs calibrate their scorer against adopter-labelled examples before trials, report agreement, and bind the calibration file digest and minimum agreement to the evaluator configuration. `check` refuses rubric scores imported from harness records until their scorer has a verifiable calibration path (Story 1.21; follow-up Story 1.40).
- `tea-evaluate` imports deterministic promptfoo assertion results through its existing command evaluator contract (Story 1.20). A disposable summarizer fixture proves that a clean summary passes and an omitted required item is caught, with each failed judgment citing observed stdout. `npm run test:evaluate-promptfoo` covers JSONL component results, single-assertion and error fallbacks, the Node engine floor and the full check, preflight, run and score pipeline. Scoped licence evidence and an exact optional-SDK verifier keep the supply-chain gate active for promptfoo's dependency graph.
- `tea-evaluate` runs a calling agent's own command in a disposable copy and judges its stdout tool-call trajectory with AgentEvals' deterministic strict matcher (Story 1.19). The adopter-owned evaluator imports its pass or fail judgment through the existing mapping contract; the clean control passes, a wrong-tool rules mutation is caught, and an always-pass evaluator leaves the defect uncaught. `npm run test:evaluate-tool-use` joins the default test chain and verifies the installed AgentEvals `LICENSE` text that supports the licence gate's MIT exception.
- `tea-evaluate` evaluates a multi-step workflow over its own interface kind, binding a later step to a value an earlier step produced (Story 1.18, AD-4).
  An interaction plan step's `{ "captured": "/interactions/<stepId>/<channel>/<key>" }` binding is sent as the value that pointer resolves to on the observation the named step recorded in the same arm, read by eval-quality's own `makeResolveOperand`, so the record's `callInputs` carry the identifier the earlier step minted in that trial. Principal and matcher bindings are supported in Story 1.30.
  A step runs after the step its `after` clause names and after every step its captured bindings read, otherwise in plan order, and each observation's `sequence` follows the order the steps were issued in.
  A step whose `after` step was not issued, one of whose captured values the earlier observation lacks, or one whose captured value the request cannot carry as printed (a header or environment value that is no string, a value holding a `__proto__` key, a header value holding a control character or one past U+00FF, a path value that is `.` or `..`, a command argument, option or environment value holding a NUL character), is not issued and records no observation; the arm's evidence lists it in `steps` as `skipped` with its reason (`after-step-not-issued`, `captured-value-absent`, `captured-value-unsendable`), a trial's records and manifest count only the calls it made, and eval-quality reads the missing observation as it reads any absent evidence, so a seeded probe whose trials never issued the step is not `caught`.
  A plan whose capture and `after` edges form a cycle stops `preflight` and `run` with `eval-quality compile`'s own exit 4 and `binding-cycle` message before any arm runs.
  The reference's new `## The interaction plan` section states the binding kinds the runtime sends, the step order and the steps it does not issue.
  The README's count of the `npm test` chain reads eighty-nine.
- `npm run test:evaluate-workflow`, chained into `npm test`, runs `check`, `preflight`, `run` and `score` over a loopback record-store fixture (`test/fixtures/evaluate-workflow/`, a `copy` workspace) whose `create` mints a fresh identifier on every run: the read-back step is issued after `create` with the identifier that trial's `create` printed, the clean arm resolves `passed-clean-control` and the mutated arm, whose `create` stores the record under another identifier than the one it prints, resolves `caught`; a trial whose `create` prints no identifier issues no read-back and scores no `caught`, and a cyclic plan passes compile's exit 4 through.

- A historical probe qualifies against two deployments of a target reachable only over HTTP, where no worktree can launch it (Story 1.32, AD-8).
  Its qualification names, in place of `fixCommit`, `deployments`: a `preFix` and a `fix` deployment, each the `release` identifier it runs and the `origins` (`scheme://host[:port]`) each HTTP interface of the registry answers at.
  An HTTP registry entry authorizes those origins in its new `deployments` list; before either arm runs, eval-quality's `evaluateTarget` decides which of the entry's authorizations allows each origin, and that one authorization is the arm's whole policy for the interface, so a redirect to another origin is denied and no other arm's policy holds a deployment.
  A deployment none of them allows refuses the probe, naming each authorization's denial in eval-quality's words, in `run.json`'s `refused` and `refused/<probeId>.json`, the rest of the run going on; an origin whose scheme, host or port no authorization admits is refused before its host is resolved, and one whose host an authorization admits and that does not resolve within the entry's `maxElapsedMs` and the 15 seconds a port call gets beyond it stops the run with exit 12.
  The fail-before arm runs against the pre-fix deployment and must be violated, the pass-after arm against the post-fix one and must hold (exit 11 otherwise); the defect's manifestation-witness leg and the trials of the arm `historical:<pre-fix release>` reach the pre-fix deployment, `run.json`'s `deployments` names the arm's origins, and no server starts for a call to a deployment.
  The qualified probe records `artifactDigest` as the digest of the pre-fix release identifier and `fixCommitDigest` as that of the post-fix one; the route needs no git history.
  `check` refuses under `historical` a probe naming neither or both of `fixCommit` and `deployments`, one deployment without the other, one release for both, a deployment beside a registry entry that is not an HTTP entry, origins that are not spelled exactly as an http or https origin for each HTTP interface of the registry and no other, and a pre-fix origin that reaches a post-fix one; it holds each deployment origin's `host` and, under `auth` over `http`, its addresses to the rules of the entry's own (`registry`).
  Two probes on one historical arm label at two targets, or on two labels that differ only in letter case, exit 10; the evidence of a leg or trial that reached a deployment names its origins.
- A deployment-routed historical probe holds each deployment to the release it reports (Story 1.38, AD-7, AD-8).
  Each `deployments.preFix` and `deployments.fix` item also names `report`, `{ operationId, pointer }`: an operation of the contract's `api` interface and an RFC 6901 JSON pointer into the JSON body of its answer.
  Before either arm runs, `preflight` and `run` send that request to each deployment through the evaluation's HTTP port, with no bound inputs and under the same eval-quality policy decision every call meets, and read the string at the pointer of a 2xx answer.
  A string other than the declared `release` refuses the probe with both identifiers named in `run.json`'s `refused` and `refused/<probeId>.json`, the clean control still running, so the digests a qualified probe records name the releases each deployment reported before the arms ran.
  A request the policy denies, and an answer with no string at the pointer (a status other than 2xx, a body the port reads as no JSON, or anything but a string at the pointer), refuse the probe with their reason; a call that reaches no answer or passes a ceiling of the registry entry stops the run with exit 12 as any call does.
  The report call is no trial and records no evidence artifact; `run.json`'s `releases` records each qualified probe's declared and reported identifier for both sides.
  `check` refuses under `historical` a `report` naming an operation the contract does not declare on an `api` interface the registry serves, one the contract marks as changing state, one that needs an input (a path parameter or a required key in any channel), or a pointer that is no JSON pointer; a deployment with no `report` fails the `schema` rule.
  The HTTP fixture's grader gains a `GET /release` route that answers the release it was started with.
- `tea-evaluate` evaluates an HTTP service, an AI feature or any web application, through an `api` interface (Story 1.11, AD-4).
  The Evaluate skill ships two templates an evaluation renders unchanged into `adapter/`: `http-probe-port.mjs`, an HTTP port whose default export holds only address, auth and transport configuration and whose every allow or deny decision is eval-quality's `evaluateTarget`, once per request and once per redirect hop, and `http-probe-port.conformance.mjs`, which runs eval-quality's environment-probe conformance suite over the port against a loopback stub it starts and closes itself, so it needs no deployed target and no secret.
  A registry entry with `kind: "api"` carries eval-quality's HTTP authorization fields, one of a deployed `port` or a `server` the runtime starts from the workspace for each call, on the port it reports or a free port, and an optional `auth` header holding the host's value for its key; a started service starts only once the port's policy has allowed the call, through eval-quality's command mechanism in a process group of its own, and ends with the call.
  A started service that does not accept a connection within its `readyTimeoutMs` stops the run with exit 12, and the cause names the last attempt's error code, `ECONNREFUSED` while nothing listens or `EADDRNOTAVAIL` on a host with no local port left to connect from.
  `tea-evaluate` starts the port file as a Node process of its own for each call, whose last lines hand the port to TeA's host, so the adopter's code runs apart from the run's state; the host speaks to the runtime on file descriptor 3, so what the port prints on its standard output and error stays its own and is quoted when a call fails; `preflight` and `run` exit 10 when the port does not answer as TeA's host, and 12 when its process cannot start or answer in time; a port that breaks the protocol during a call exits 12, as does an answer eval-quality's own `ProbeObservation` parser does not read, which is never judged as the target's behavior.
  Every leg, qualification arm, trial, gameability arm and sealed-brief bridge call of an `api` interface goes through that port, so eval-quality denies an unlisted address (`address-not-authorized`), method, scheme or interface before any request is sent or any service starts, and the denial's `reason` is recorded.
  A record carries a request's `path`, `query`, `header` and `body` inputs and the answer's status, headers and body; a started service's environment values and the auth value are scrubbed from every answer, fault message and cause, as written and lowercased (a URL lowercases the host a redirect names), and a failure quotes the end of what the port's process or a started service printed only once the whole of it is scrubbed, so the cut never leaves part of a secret; each call's port receives the auth value of the call's own interface alone; a gameability probe's degenerate response answers an HTTP step with `{ status, headers?, body? }` through the same port with nothing sent, every host of the request resolving to its entry's first address, so eval-quality's own host check decides each spelling of a host as on a real arm.
  `check` refuses, under `registry`, two HTTP entries for one interface, an HTTP entry for an interface of another kind, a `host` a URL spells otherwise, letter case aside (the finding names the URL's spelling, the hostname the port hands eval-quality's policy), and an `auth` header sent over `http` to an address eval-quality's `staysOnHost` says leaves the host, so the credential never crosses the network in clear text (the finding says to serve such a target over `https`, with `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` naming a private certificate authority), under `schema` an entry naming both or neither of `port` and `server`, and under the new `adapter` rule a folder whose registry declares an HTTP target and holds no regular `adapter/http-probe-port.mjs`.
- A started HTTP service can report the port it bound itself (Story 1.37, delivered in Story 1.11's pull request).
  A registry `server` naming `portFileEnvironmentKey` receives `0` in `portEnvironmentKey` and the path of a file in a private directory, binds a port the system chooses and writes its number to that file; the runtime sends only once the file names a port that accepts a connection while the service runs, and TeA's host probes the call again at that port, so eval-quality's policy decides there and no other process can answer on it.
  A service that writes no port within `readyTimeoutMs`, or writes anything other than a port number, stops the run with exit 12, and the file's directory is on the run's scratch list, so a signal removes it.
  The runtime reads that file without following a link and without waiting on it, so a service that puts a link, a named pipe or a file longer than 16 bytes in its place stops the run with exit 12 at once, and its timers and signal handlers keep running; it reads `adapter/http-probe-port.mjs` the same way when it checks that file's digest before each call, so a port file replaced by a pipe or a link during a run fails the call with `port-failure`.
  A server without that key keeps the chosen-port handoff, whose window between the port's release and the service's bind the reference now states.
  An answer the port gives before the call's service is ready is refused (exit 12), and `check` refuses (`registry`) a `portFileEnvironmentKey` equal to `portEnvironmentKey` and either key named in `environmentKeys`.
  The HTTP fixture's service reports its port, which ends a `test:evaluate-api` flake under parallel load: another process took the chosen port, and a run stopped with "another process listens on 127.0.0.1 port ..." or sealed no trial set.
- `npm run test:evaluate-api`, chained into `npm test`, runs the port template in process (a counting evaluator, redirects, caps, auth), its conformance file, and `check`, `preflight`, `run` and `score` over a loopback HTTP fixture (`test/fixtures/evaluate-api/`, a `copy` workspace): the clean arm resolves `passed-clean-control` and the mutated arm `caught`, an unlisted address is denied `address-not-authorized` at the qualification, a leg, a trial and a bridge call with no service started, and a sealed-brief agent and a gameability probe run over the service.
  `npm run test:port-totality` now names it as the check that runs eval-quality's `environment-probe` conformance arm.

- `tea-evaluate` evaluates a stdio MCP tool server (Story 1.10, AD-4).
  A registry entry with `kind: "mcp"` names the server (`target`, `targetArgs`), the `tools` a call may name and the `environmentKeys` whose host values it starts with; the runtime turns each into eval-quality's `McpTargetAuthorization` for the workspace a call runs in, checked by eval-quality's `parseMcpTargetPolicy`, and calls the server through its `createMcpAdapter`, one stdio session per call.
  Every leg, qualification arm, trial and sealed-brief bridge call of an `mcp` interface goes through that authorization, so eval-quality denies an unlisted tool (`tool-not-authorized`) or interface before the server starts.
  A record carries a tool call's arguments as `callInputs.arguments`, its structured result as `responseBody` and its error flag as `responseStatus`, with the server's environment values scrubbed from strings, object keys and numbers, as written and as one or two levels of JSON escaping write them (`JSON.stringify`'s body, `\/`, and `\uXXXX` for non-ASCII characters and for `<`, `>` and `&` in either hex case); a gameability probe's degenerate response answers a tool-call step with `{ isError, structuredResult }`.
  A server's `targetArgs` may hold no absolute path, none after `=`, no `file:` URL and no `..` segment, the forms that would run the adopter's live tree in place of the workspace's copy; a sealed-brief agent's tool call whose arguments carry a `__proto__` key, which eval-quality's parser drops, is refused unsent.
  A server that cannot start, refuses its handshake, crosses a ceiling or ends its session during a call stops the run with exit 12, so a mutation that crashes the server is not caught on an `mcp` interface until Story 1.35.
  `check` refuses, under `registry`, an interface named by a command and a tool server, an entry of another kind than the contract's interface, and tool servers eval-quality's `parseMcpTargetPolicy` refuses (two for one interface included), under `reference` an `evaluation.json` `interface` the contract declares no interface of, and under `gameability` a degenerate response of the other kind than its step.
- Every denial `tea-evaluate` records (a leg's `faults/` file, a qualification's or a trial's fault, a sealed-brief agent's bridge calls) keeps the `reason` eval-quality's policy gave beside the `forbidden-target` code, for a command and a tool call alike, and `preflight` and `run` name it when they exit 10; a fault a target could not run under keeps the adapter's cause, scrubbed, a value the cause cuts short included, and the exit-12 message names it; a sealed-brief trial names a bridge call's fault ahead of the agent's own failure, and both when both happened.
- `npm run test:evaluate-mcp`, chained into `npm test`, runs `check`, `preflight`, `run` and `score` over a loopback MCP server fixture (`test/fixtures/evaluate-mcp/`, a `copy` workspace): the clean arm resolves `passed-clean-control` and the mutated arm `caught`, a removed tool is denied `tool-not-authorized` with no server started, a sealed-brief agent's listed, unlisted and undeclared tools are routed through the arm's authorization, a gameability probe over the tool call is caught with nothing started, and a server that hangs mid-call is torn down at its ceiling with no process left.

- `tea-evaluate run` and `score` take any evaluation layer through one import contract (Story 1.17, AD-21).
  `evaluation.json`'s new `evaluator` chooses the kind: `deterministic` (the default, TeA's `resolveCheck` evaluator with its rubric judge), `command` (an executable of yours under `evaluator/`, run once per trial under TeA's agent supervisor with `{ sealedBrief, observations }` on stdin), `sealed-brief-agent` (an agent through TeA's adapters that reads the sealed brief alone and acts on the target through a vendor-neutral stdio MCP bridge, one tool per interface the brief names) or `records` (the sealed run records your own harness wrote, validated and copied byte for byte).
  A `command` evaluator and a sealed-brief agent answer in judgment rows (`key`, `outcome` `pass`, `fail` or `score`, cited `observationIds`, a verbatim `quote` with its `quoteChannel`, `confidence` and `comment`), converted through `evaluator/mapping.json` alone into `Finding`-valid defect findings, oracle dispositions and judge results; the runtime checks no quote or citation, so eval-quality's own ingest decides them.
  A `fail` row files its finding for the first behavior of the probe under trial that declares the row's oracle, as the deterministic evaluator does, so an oracle two behaviors declare is judged for either through one key.
  An evaluator that crashes, exits other than 0, outlives its `timeoutMs` (its process group is stopped) or answers outside the contract (a lone surrogate in a string included) yields no record and exit 12, and what it printed is kept byte for byte under `runs/<invocationId>/evaluator/`.
  In a git repository the evaluation layer is the files git tracks under `evaluator/`, so an untracked or ignored file there moves no digest; a command evaluator runs in place from `evaluator/`, so a package in your project's `node_modules` resolves as it does outside a run, and a cache it writes there must be gitignored or the adopter-tree check stops the run.
  `run` reads the layer's files before anything runs, again before each launch of the evaluator and after each trial, and exits 12 with no record when one is gone, changed or added, so the digests name the bytes that ran.
  The private directories a run makes for an evaluator, a judge or the bridge (its token's configuration and its socket included) are removed however the run ends, an interrupting signal included, write bits restored first so a read-only directory left in one cannot stop the removal; one that still cannot be removed is reported by name and every workspace is removed after it.
  The bridge authorizes every call through the registry before anything launches, a gameability arm's calls included (denied there exactly as on a real arm, with nothing launched), records an authorized call that matches one contract operation as an `evaluator-chosen` observation, sends a call's standard input as the agent wrote it, keeps an unmatched or denied call in the trial's evidence, refuses unsent a call carrying the trial's answer nonce, holds the agent to the contract's `budgets.maxToolCalls`, stops calls once the agent ends, and admits one connection, whose token reaches it through its environment; this release's registry holds command targets only, so eval-quality denies `api` and `mcp` calls at the interface (Stories 1.10 and 1.11).
  `claude` (verified live: no built-in tool, the bridge alone, no user or project settings, no saved transcript) and `custom` run as sealed-brief agents.
  The evaluator configuration records the kind and, for a row-converting evaluator, the `evaluator/` tree digest, the executable's digest, and the `evaluation.json` wiring (arguments, model, timeout) under `decodingParameters`, so a changed evaluator changes the scoring version; a deterministic run's configuration gains `tea.evaluatorKind`, so its digest differs once from the release before.
- `runAgentAsync` and `runSupervised` in `cli/lib/run-agent.js` run an agent or a command under the agent supervisor without blocking the event loop.
- `tea-evaluate check` gains `evaluator` (a mapping missing or binding what the contract does not declare, levels off a criterion's anchored scale, an unbound rubric criterion, a link or special file under `evaluator/`, a submodule there (named as one), a mapping or command git does not track, a non-executable command, a sealed-brief agent on an adapter with no bridged run or with no `evaluator.modelSnapshot`, a missing records directory; a mapping off its schema is reported under `schema`), and its `judge` rule binds only the deterministic evaluator, refusing a `judge` block beside any other kind as unused.
- `npm run test:evaluate-evaluators`, chained into `npm test`, runs each evaluator kind end to end over the real eval-quality (every row shape scoring `passed-clean-control` and `caught`, an unwitnessed quote read as Invalid by eval-quality, each answer outside the contract and a hung evaluator exiting 12, a sealed-brief stub agent through the bridge, a harness's records passed through byte for byte) and plants an evaluation framework import under a copy of `cli/` to prove the dependency-direction gate refuses it; `test:evaluate-boundaries` also refuses an evaluation framework named anywhere under `cli/`.

- `tea-evaluate run` measures gameability and historical probes, and judges rubrics (Story 1.9, AD-7, AD-8).
  A gameability probe launches nothing: its degenerate response is committed at `corpus/gameability/<probeId>.json` (one `{ stdout, stderr, exitCode }` per plan step, a new runtime-owned schema), `preflight` and `run` answer the interaction plan from it as a synthetic observation, and its `naiveOracle` (an oracle of another behavior, a new field of the committed probe) must hold over it while the oracle of the probe's own behavior is violated (exit 11 otherwise); the naive-satisfied and disciplined-rejected evidence lands under `qualification/<probeId>/`, the probe is materialized as eval-quality's `gameability` probe, and its trials run on the arm `gameability:<probeId>`.
  A historical probe names the `fixCommit` that removed its natural defect: `preflight` and `run` qualify it in a worktree at the fix commit's parent, where its oracles must be violated (`fail-before.json`), and one at the fix commit, where they must hold (`pass-after.json`), route its manifestation-witness leg to the parent, and run its trials on the arm `historical:<preFixSha>`; the probe records the tracked tree at the parent as its `artifactDigest` and the digest of the fix commit's id as `fixCommitDigest`.
  `fixCommit` is a hexadecimal commit id, never a moving ref; one that names no commit in a full-history repository, or that resolves through a branch or tag of the same spelling, exits 10 naming the probe.
  A historical probe with no revisions to address (a copy workspace, a shallow clone missing the fix or its parent, a fix commit that is not an ancestor of the evaluated commit or has no parent, or a revision, before or at the fix, where the target cannot run: its worktree lacks `launch.root` or holds a submodule under it, the skill root is not a directory, or a registry target is missing or not executable, links followed and provisioned copies included) is refused with its reason in `run.json`'s `refused` and `refused/<probeId>.json`, stays out of the preflight and the trial sets, and the rest of the run goes on; `score` prints each refused probe with its reason and records it in its aggregate `score.json`.
  This route addresses revisions as git commits launched from worktrees; Story 1.32 adds the deployment route for a target reachable only as a remote deployment.
  When the contract declares a rubric, a judge runs once per trial through TeA's agent adapters, wired by `evaluation.json`'s new `judge` and named as a fixed condition by `policy/evaluator-conditions.json`'s new `judge.modelSnapshot`; it receives the instruction template (which marks the evidence as data whose instructions are never followed), each rubric's anchors and penalties, and the evidence each criterion points at, never the contract, and answers inside one `<judge-answer nonce="...">` block carrying a 128-bit nonce drawn after the target ran, the only part of its reply that is read, so a scores object a target prints never becomes a score; every record of the trial carries its scores as `judgeResults` (a reply with no block carrying the call's nonce or with several, a block that is not a JSON object with a `scores` list, an off-scale score or a note past `maxLength` is recorded as `score: null`, which eval-quality reads as Invalid; a judge that cannot answer, times out or writes into its read-only directory yields no record and exit 12, its streams kept; an interrupting signal during the call ends the run by that signal). `check` refuses an adapter that cannot run read-only as the judge. The evaluator configuration records `judgeConfiguration: { modelSnapshot, systemPromptDigest }`; a contract with no rubric makes no judge call and keeps `judgeConfiguration: null`.
- `tea-evaluate check` gains `gameability` (a gameability probe off its class or route, one that seeds a defect, a naive oracle of the probe's own behavior, or a degenerate response that is missing, answers a step the plan does not declare, leaves one unanswered or exits an infrastructure code), `historical` (a historical probe without `expectedClean: false`, with no defect, or with one that is not `natural`), `evaluator-conditions` also refusing `modelSnapshot: none` beside a `systemPromptDigest` other than the empty byte string's, and `judge` (a rubric with no `judge` or no judge model, a judge adapter, command or model TeA's agent adapters cannot run, or a `judge` block beside a contract that declares no rubric); `arms` takes `historical` and `gameability`, and a historical or gameability probe needs `policy/scoring-policy.json`.
- `npm run test:evaluate-arms`, chained into `npm test`, runs each new arm end to end over the real eval-quality: a gameability arm that launches nothing and scores `caught`, a historical arm across a real fix commit whose witness leg and trials run at the parent and score `caught`, a refused historical probe in a one-commit repository, and a stub rubric judge called once per trial, never without a rubric, and failing with exit 12; every materialized probe passes eval-quality's `qualifyProbe` with no failure code.

- `tea-evaluate run` measures an evaluation as sealed trial sets (Story 1.8, AD-7).
  It runs the whole preflight, then qualifies each clean control on one clean arm and runs every arm a probe needs `trials` times, the clean arm for the clean controls and one mutated arm per mutation, each trial in a workspace of its own that reproduces the pristine one.
  The deterministic evaluator judges every trial through eval-quality's `resolveCheck`: a disposition for every oracle, and a `defect` finding per violated oracle of the behaviors the probe discharges, quoting a cited observation verbatim; the trial's observations are `evaluator-chosen`, and every record of a set carries the set's one recommendation.
  Each probe gets one trial set under `runs/<invocationId>/trial-sets/<probeId>/`: a Sealed Run Record per trial (one `runId`, `trialIndex` 1..N, `mode: contract-scoring`, `conditionArm` `clean` or `mutated:<mutationId>`) and an isolation manifest from the workspaces and commands the trials were granted and the commands the runtime ran (the runtime observes no file-system access, so it records no observed mount), with one evaluator configuration for the run carrying the seal's `sealedBriefDigest`; every artifact is checked against eval-quality's published schema before it is written.
  A trial that exits an infrastructure code or is stopped by a signal from outside yields no record and the run exits 12, as does a change to the project after any trial or after the last write; `run.json` records the digests of every file `score` reads, runner and model, trial count, duration, how the invocation ended (an interrupting signal included), `completed: true` as the run's last write, only once the trial sets are sealed and verified (a retraction the runtime cannot make is named in the exit message), and `dirty: true` when the evaluation folder holds uncommitted work.
  The runtime does not sandbox the target's file system (Story 1.31), so it guards its own writes to the run directory: every directory and file in it is created afresh without following a link, from inside a directory the runtime made, held open until the command ends (so its inode number cannot pass to a directory the target makes), and confirmed before and after the write by device, inode and the path the system reports for it, `run.json` is replaced by renaming a new file over it, engine stages write into a private temp directory whose output is copied in, every file is read back, without blocking, only with the bytes the runtime wrote, and the run directory must hold exactly those entries before the preflight verdict, after the trials and before `run.json` says completed (exit 12 otherwise); a link a target plants there, or a directory it replaces or moves into your project with a link left in its place, stops the run with exit 12 before the runtime writes through it.
  Probes carry AD-7's digests: the evaluated commit, the `targetArtifact` bytes, and the tracked tree of the skill or target root at that commit, the evaluation folder left out.
- `tea-evaluate score` scores a completed run's trial sets through `eval-quality score`, once per probe, with every trial's `--record`, the set's isolation manifest and the run's evaluator configuration, after checking every input against eval-quality's published schemas, `trial-sets.json` against the runtime's own, and each set against its run: the runId the run derives, references inside the run directory, and the digests `run.json` recorded for the compiled contract, the policy, the preflight verdict, the evaluator configuration and every probe, record and isolation manifest (exit 10 before any engine call otherwise), each read as a regular file without blocking, so a FIFO or a link there is a finding; a run whose `run.json` does not say it completed exits 64.
  `score` holds each file to the digest `run.json` recorded; a process that can write the run directory after the run, a target's leftover process included, can rewrite both, and Story 1.31 closes that.
  Each call's argv, exit code, stdout and stderr are kept under `runs/<invocationId>/scores/<scoreInvocationId>/<probeId>/` with its evidence artifact, and the command exits with the most severe call's own exit.
  An absent isolation manifest is passed on as absent, so eval-quality reads the set as Invalid.
- `policy/evaluator-conditions.json`, a runtime-owned schema: the model a run uses and the digest of its system prompt, recorded in every run's evaluator configuration; a run with no such file records `modelSnapshot: none` and the digest of the empty byte string.
- `tea-evaluate check` gains `trials` (below the scoring policy's `minimumTrialCount`), `arms` (a probe whose arm `arms` does not declare, or a declared arm no probe runs on), `mutation-route` (a `controlled-mutation` probe that seeds no defect) and `evaluator-conditions` (a `tea-skill-runner` registry entry with no `policy/evaluator-conditions.json`, or one naming `none`), and holds that file to its schema.
- `tea-evaluate preflight`'s qualification arms, like `run`'s trials, treat a target step a signal from outside stopped (hang-up, interrupt, quit, kill or terminate) as a target that could not run (exit 12); a step that crashes by its own signal stays an observation its oracles judge.
- The Evaluate skill's `assets/` ships a scoring-policy template and an evaluator-conditions template whose threshold and model values are left `null` for the adopter, and an evaluation-folder `.gitignore` template that ignores `runs/`; TeA's own `.gitignore` ignores `runs/` under `test/evaluations/*/` and `test/fixtures/evaluate*/`.
- `npm run test:evaluate-run`, chained into `npm test`, runs `tea-evaluate run` and `score` end to end on the verdict fixture and asserts `passed-clean-control` and `caught` over three trials with a comparable strength vector, the record, manifest and configuration shapes, AD-7's digests, byte-identical evidence from a direct `eval-quality score` on a passing and a FAIL run, an Invalid score when the manifest is omitted, each call's persisted streams through a logging shim, and exit 12 for an infrastructure trial.
- `tea-evaluate preflight` qualifies every seeded probe on the `controlled-mutation` route in a disposable workspace before its legs run, and proves the rollback (Story 1.7, AD-8).
  AD-8's six steps run in a workspace of the probe's own: the clean arm must pass, the mutation is applied, the mutated arm must fail, the original bytes are restored with their mode, their digest and mode must equal the pre-mutation ones, and the clean arm must pass again within the scoring policy's `reExecutionCap`.
  An arm is one pass over the contract's interaction plan with its literal bindings, judged by a deterministic evaluator that resolves each oracle of the probe's behaviors through eval-quality's `resolveCheck`.
  `rollbackVerified` is the conjunction of the matching digest and the re-pass, never a literal; the evidence (`baseline-pass.json`, `mutated-fail.json`, and `rollback.json` with `preDigest`, `mutatedDigest` and `restoredDigest`) lands under `runs/<invocationId>/qualification/<probeId>/`, and the qualified probe, checked against eval-quality's probe schema and qualification gate, under `runs/<invocationId>/probes/` once the adopter's project is confirmed unchanged after the legs.
  The qualified probe reaches `eval-quality preflight`, and its manifestation witness's leg runs in a mutated workspace of its own while every other leg runs in the pristine one; each observation records the workspace and working directory it ran in (AD-6).
  A `find` text that does not occur exactly once (overlapping occurrences counted) exits 10, a baseline that does not pass or a mutation that does not fail exits 11, and a workspace, restore or infrastructure failure, or a restored workspace that does not pass again (an unfit harness), exits 12, each with no qualified probe written.
- `tea-evaluate preflight` runs in AD-8's workspaces: a detached `git worktree` at `HEAD` for a git target (hooks disabled, its entry removed afterwards), a temp copy identified by its tree digest for a `copy` workspace or a target outside git, and a temp copy of the working tree under the new `--from-working-tree`, the one workspace recorded as `dirty: true`.
  Every later workspace of a run reproduces the first (the same commit, or a copy of the first copy), and the evaluation folder is left out of all of them, so a target cannot read which defect a mutation plants.
  `runs/<invocationId>/run.json` records the versions, the commit (`null` for a copy), `dirty` and every workspace that ran legs.
  Each `workspace.provision` directory is copied in as a copy-on-write clone where the file system offers one, with its write bits removed, so a write under it fails unless the writer restores them; a provisioned directory that is a symbolic link is refused, and a symbolic link inside one that leads out of the project is refused like any other.
  `preflight` reads the adopter's project before the workspaces are made and after the qualification and the legs (in git: `git status`, the content of every path it names, every ref, and the common git directory without its object store and bookkeeping, which a worktree shares; outside git: the tree digest) and exits 12 on any change, with the probe list it handed the CLI removed.
  A worktree whose `launch.root` holds a submodule is refused with exit 12, since the worktree checks it out empty, and a `TMPDIR` inside the repository is refused for every workspace kind unless the repository ignores it.
  The rollback cycle records where the `targetArtifact`'s directory really lies, holds it open for the whole cycle, and refuses (exit 12) to write or read it once an arm has made that path resolve anywhere else, `launch.root` swapped for a link included, has replaced the directory, has moved it behind a link, or has hard-linked the file; the mutation and the restore each write a new file, so neither can reach another path's data.
- `tea-evaluate check` requires `policy/scoring-policy.json` once a probe takes the `controlled-mutation` route, and holds a policy that is present to eval-quality's scoring-policy schema and version.
- `npm run test:evaluate-mutation`, chained into `npm test`, runs `tea-evaluate preflight` over temp git projects built from `test/fixtures/evaluate/mutation/`, whose verdict command prints the SHA-256 of its own `targetArtifact`, and asserts the worktree, the dirty flag, the three digests, the leg routing, the read-only provisioning and each failure exit, with the project's `git status` and every file unchanged.
- `npm run test:evaluate-boundaries` gains a `rollback-literal` rule over `cli/`, and holds the moves of the live harness's leg cache and per-leg staging (`cachingPort`, `stageDirectories`) into `cli/lib/evaluate/workspace.js` and of the mutate, measure, restore cycle (`runMutationCycle`) into `cli/lib/evaluate/mutation.js`; `test/eval-contract-strength.js` and `test/test-automate-eval-fixture.js` keep only TeA data.
- `tea-skill-runner` (`cli/skill-runner.js`) ships as a new bin: the generic runner an Evaluate evaluation registers for a skill (Story 1.6, AD-4).
  It takes the skill from an explicit `--skill-root`, which with its `SKILL.md` must resolve inside the working directory, reads the prompt on stdin, tells the agent where the skill is, and runs one turn through TeA's agent adapters, so vendor knowledge stays in `cli/lib/agent-adapters.js` and `--agent` has no default.
  It never probes an install location.
  It exits 2 for a usage error (a prompt that is not valid UTF-8 included) and 3 to 6 for the infrastructure classes of `cli/lib/runner-exit-codes.js`, and an unexpected error or a standard output closed before the reply was written exits 4, so its registry entry declares `infrastructureExitCodes` 3 to 6.
- `tea-evaluate preflight --evaluation <path>` drives an evaluation's preflight against the real target (Story 1.6, AD-6).
  It runs `check`, makes a disposable workspace of `launch.root` the legs run in (see the Story 1.7 entries), runs `eval-quality compile` and `seal` over the run's own copy of the contract, drives the legs through eval-quality's `runPreflight` with a recording port over the registry's command-line adapter, writes every observation under `runs/<invocationId>/observations/`, and passes through the exit of `eval-quality preflight --observations ... --run-id <invocationId>` over the persisted files.
  The library's own verdict is discarded.
  The copy leaves out `.git` and the evaluation's `runs/`, points every symbolic link inside the target at the copy (each link resolved by the system, so a `..` after a link climbs from the link's target), and is removed when the command ends or is interrupted by `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP` or `SIGQUIT`; a link out of `launch.root`, a FIFO, socket or device, and a temp directory inside `launch.root` are refused with exit 12.
  Each `workspace.provision` directory is copied into the workspace read-only (see the Story 1.7 entry).
  `--evaluation` resolves to the folder's real path, so `launch.root` resolves the same through a symbolic link to the folder.
  A request's environment is recorded as keys only, and the injected values are scrubbed from observations.
  A leg the registry does not authorize exits 10 with its fault under `runs/<invocationId>/faults/`; a leg that cannot run, a target that cannot launch, a stage that cannot run or exits with a code eval-quality does not document for that stage, and an evaluation holding a probe that seeds a defect on a route other than `controlled-mutation` exit 12.
  `runs/` gets a `.gitignore` on first use, and `engine/<stage>.json` records each stage's executable, argv, exit and output, including a `TEA_EVALUATE_ENGINE_CLI` substitution.
- `evaluation.json`'s `launch` gains its shape: `root`, the evaluated project relative to the evaluation folder, and `skillRoot`, required for a skill target.
