  The shard weights were refreshed from the measured CI seconds of two CI runs, so the heaviest scripts (`test:evaluate-confinement` 861 s, `test:evaluate-partition-plans` 647 s, `test:evaluate-evaluators` 628 s) are placed by what CI measures under coverage.
  `npm run test:evaluate-ci-repositories` still runs every tier in one command for a person; CI does not run it.
- An edit run of `bmad-testarch-ci` renames an evaluation job whose id the rules no longer give (Story 1.93, AD-11).
  Section 4 of `steps-c/step-03b-render-evaluation-plans.md` told a second run to rewrite each marker job under the id item 1 of section 3 gives now, and the live edit runs of Story 1.93 kept the id `evaluation-evals-ledger-pr` of a job written while the repository held two plans, with a plan folder in it and its artifact named for it.
  The section is now a list: match the jobs by marker, work out the id the rules give now, rename the job and its artifact name to it, and keep no job under the old id.
  The live edit run that follows the new text rewrites the job under `evaluation-pr` and `evaluation-pr-runs`, leaves the hand-written job and the create run's checkpoint untouched, and scores 12 of 12.
- The AI-feature evaluation and the two repositories of the CI stage that copy it pass their own CI tiers (Story 1.98, CAP-11, CAP-12, AD-10).
  Before, a clean copy that accepted its baseline ended red on eval-quality 7.1.0: `ci --tier pr` exited 11 in both repositories, because `oracle-agreement` read `disagrees` on P-006 O-004, P-012 O-004 and P-009 O-003 and O-004, and `ci --tier release` exited 2, because `twin-run` and `held-out` read `no-eligible-probe` for the zero-action floor and `held-out` for the gameability floor too (`scheduled` of `nightly-deploy` exited 0 with the same warnings).
  Every tier of both plans now exits 0 with no warning over a committed baseline.
  P-006 (mutation M-002) and P-012 (M-006) violate O-004 beside O-002, so each probe declares a second defect, D-009 and D-010, for B-004 with its own manifestation witness.
  Each witness reads the reason the mutation produces, since two mutations that reject the same input differ in their reason (`too-short` for M-002, `accepted` for M-006) and a witness that fires on the other mutation's leg fails `seeded-faults-scoped`.
  eval-quality marks a finding that the probe's one defect signature does not admit as an unwitnessed detection claim and scores the run Invalid, so each signature is an `any` over the relations of its two witnesses.
  P-009's degenerate answer differed from the correct server on the missing, nonstring and strict steps and so violated O-003 and O-004; it now matches the correct server on every step except the restricted one it games.
  The zero-action floor is gone: the contract's four behaviors each respond to a request, no mandatory action has a missing-action defect probe to expose, and eval-quality leaves every `expectedClean` probe out of the strength vector, so no clean control can fill that floor.
  The held-out partition gains P-015, a gameability probe for B-004 (strict mode reported, the doubled minimum not applied), so the gameability floor holds there too.
  Both repositories commit a `baseline/` recorded by `compare --accept` over a clean copy, and the Story 1.24 replay bundles are recorded again on 7.1.0.
  `test:evaluate-ci-repositories` runs the tiers `pr`, `merge`, `scheduled` and `release` that each plan places over a copy of each repository, and expects exit 0, no warning, an agreeing oracle and a floor decision of `meets` for the classes the evaluation declares.
  `test:evaluate-authoring` holds both Story 1.24 replays, the AI-feature and the test-review evaluation, to agreeing oracles, a defect for every behavior a mutation violates, a degenerate answer that violates only its own behavior's oracle, and an eligible probe in each partition for every declared floor.
  The behaviors a mutation or a degenerate answer violates come from the oracle outcomes of the scored trials, which hold every contract oracle with its disposition. The qualification evidence lists only the oracles of the behaviors a probe declares, so a check over it could not fail.
  Every oracle outcome must read `agrees`, so a `not-evaluable` outcome fails too.
  Per scored trial, one assertion fails for a violated behavior the probe does not declare and another for a declared behavior whose oracles the trial leaves held, each printing `trial n violates the oracles of [..] where the probe declares [..]`; the corroboration assertion runs after them.
  Run over the pre-repair bytes of `origin/main`, the checks fail by name: P-006 violates B-002 and B-004 where its defects declare B-002, P-009 violates B-001, B-003 and B-004 where it games B-001, and the test-review held-out probes P-010, P-012 and P-013 violate behaviors their defects do not declare.
  The capture records of both repositories declare the repair of `evaluation.json` as a Story 1.98 migration, which the guard in `test:evaluate-ci` reverses to the bytes the live session wrote; a file that still declares the `zero-action` floor beside `P-015` is refused.
  The test-review evaluation of Story 1.24 gets the same repair.
  Its held-out replay held 15 `disagrees` outcomes (P-010 O-002, P-012 O-002, P-013 O-001, O-002 and O-003, each over three trials), and it declared `zero-action` and `gameability` floors that its held-out partition could not fill.
  M-005 (P-010) and M-007 (P-012) add `missing-assertion` to a disabled test, which violates O-002 beside the oracle each probe declares; M-008 (P-013) refuses every valid request, which violates O-001, O-002 and O-003 beside O-004.
  Each probe now declares a defect for every behavior its mutation violates (D-014 to D-018), each with a witness that reads what its mutation answers, and a signature that admits all of its witnesses.
  The zero-action floor is gone, since the six zero-action probes are all `expectedClean` and no behavior is a mandatory action; P-015 joins the held-out partition as a gameability probe for B-003 (every valid test flagged as disabled), so the gameability floor holds there.
  The replay is recorded again on eval-quality 7.1.0 with its manifest, and every oracle outcome of both partitions reads `agrees`.
  The Evaluate skill's starter `assets/evaluation.json` declares the `defect` floor alone, and `references/corpus.md` says what a floor needs.
  Every class a floor declares needs an eligible probe in the partition `baseline/` records and in `heldOutProbes`, or the evaluation declares no floor for it, because an `expectedClean` probe never counts toward a floor and the twin run repeats the partition `baseline/` recorded (the whole corpus for a `both` baseline).
  The Workflow kind's worked corpus holds out P-008, a defect probe of B-002 (M-003, unseen reservation R-19), since its held-out P-006 is a `zero-action` defect probe and P-007, the only development probe of B-002, has to stay in development.
  With it the starter's `defect` floor has an eligible probe in both partitions of all six kinds, and `test:evaluate-guidance` checks that per kind.
  One gameability probe cannot sit in both partitions, so the guide has the adopter commit a second gameability probe beside the worked P-004 before declaring a `gameability` floor.
  An adopter who followed the starter ended at `ci --tier release` with exit 2 and `no-eligible-probe`.
  `test:evaluate-guidance` holds the starter's floor and each new sentence, and the CLI reference states the same rules.
  The CLI reference says which floors an evaluation can declare.

- A confined run builds its private git repository without writing into the project's object store, so a project and a temp directory on different filesystems work (Story 1.132, AD-7, AD-8).
  The build ran `git pack-objects --revs <store>/objects/pack/pack` inside the project's repository, which wrote its temporary pack into the project's `.git/objects/pack` and renamed it into the private store under the temp directory.
  A project under a home directory with a tmpfs `/tmp`, the default layout on Linux hosts, was refused with `Invalid cross-device link`, and a failed build left `tmp_pack_*` and `tmp_idx_*` files in the project's `objects/pack`.
  The project's repository now prints the pack (`pack-objects --stdout`, after a `rev-list --objects --missing=allow-any` walk for a partial clone) and the private repository indexes it (`index-pack --stdin`), so the pack is written on the private store's own device and the project's `objects/pack` is only read, and the build writes only the worktree's own entry under the project's `.git/worktrees/`.
  The streaming reader runs the stages as one pipeline, reads a very large history as a stream with no buffer that holds the walk, and a stage that fails ends the others and names itself and its status.
  The commit ids, the digests and the isolation golden are unchanged.
  The reference's `### File-system confinement` says the build writes nothing into your repository's object store.
  `test:evaluate-confinement` holds the build across filesystems with a read-only `objects/pack` (Linux hosts, through the home directory or `/dev/shm`; skipped with its reason where neither is on another filesystem than the temp directory), the stages in a full repository and a partial clone, a failed pack or index stage that leaves no workspace and no file in the project, and the pipeline's unit cases.
  A second workspace for the same commit now links the first one's objects on a git before 2.45 too, which does not report a ref format and has only `files`, and a case holds it on every host.

- The Windows guardian trace keeps a line when several processes append to its file at once (Story 1.113 review round 1).
  `agent-supervisor.js` appended its trace lines with one attempt and swallowed every error, so a sharing violation under load dropped a line, and the "setup race" case of `test:evaluate-preflight` failed on the `windows-agent-supervision` job although the agent had started.
  In trace mode the writer now retries `EBUSY`, `EPERM`, `EACCES` and `EMFILE` up to 50 times with a short wait, and still swallows the last error, since a diagnostic never affects supervision.
  A unit case stubs the refusals on every platform.

- A confined run over a partial clone refuses at the pack stage on every run, and the streaming git reader no longer exits 0 after a stage failed (Story 1.120, AD-7, AD-8).
  The `pack` job counted a stage as ended when its `close` event arrived, and `close` waits until the stage's standard output has been read to its end.
  The pipe into the next stage stops reading when that stage exits, so a walk whose last bytes were still unread, or whose child held the pipe open, never closed.
  The reader's event loop then ran dry and the job exited 0 with the pack never built, and the build refused later at `read-tree HEAD` with `failed to unpack tree object HEAD`.
  Linux CI showed it once.
  A loop of the case on `128680ad` in a git 2.47 container failed 1 of 40 serial runs and 12 of 60 runs eight at a time, every failure a refusal at `read-tree`.
  A stage now ends on its `exit`, so the job reports the first failed stage whatever state the pipes are in.
  The `trees` job had the same defect: a commit list paused for a `cat-file` stage that had already ended never finished, and the job exited 0 with no trees.
  The job closes the list's output when that stage fails, and a `cat-file` stage that exits 0 before the whole list was handed to it, or having answered fewer lines than it was sent, now fails the job, since it answered for part of the history.
  `npm run loop:failing-pack` repeats the case (`--runs`, `--parallel`, `--log`) and fails a run that did not exercise it.
  The `Failing-pack loop` workflow runs it serially and in parallel on Linux for a pull request that touches the pack stage or the loop (20 serial and 60 parallel runs, about 10 minutes) and on demand (200 runs in each loop by default).
  `test:evaluate-confinement` holds each fix with a walk that has exited while a child still holds its output, and with `cat-file` stages that exit unread, two with status 4 (one over a list whose output a child still holds) and one with status 0.

- Another session's commit in a second worktree does not stop a confined run, and the evaluation layer cannot write the project's git directory or its hooks directory (Story 1.112, AD-8).
  `preflight` and `run` compared every ref and the shared git state of the repository before and after, so a commit, fetch or branch in any other worktree of the same repository while a run was in flight changed the digest and ended the run with exit 12, with no qualified probe written.
  A confined run now compares the working tree, the checkout's own `HEAD` and where the checkout's git commands read their repository from (the git directory the checkout resolves to, the content of its `.git` file and whether the hooks directory `core.hooksPath` names exists), and digests no other part of the git directory.
  A tracked file edited, an untracked file created or an edit committed in the project's own checkout while the run is in flight exits 12.
  A ref, the configuration, branch tracking or an in-progress operation that changes in any other checkout of the same repository (the main checkout when the run reads a linked worktree; `git push -u`, `git worktree add -b` from a remote-tracking ref, a stopped rebase, cherry-pick, merge or `gc`) leaves the run alone, and `run.json` records `adopterTree.unchanged: true`.
  Every process of a confined run is denied a write to the project's common git directory, which is the guard: the target's profile withholds it, and the evaluation layer (a command evaluator, the HTTP port, the judge, the sealed-brief agent) runs with it read-only, through a Seatbelt deny rule on macOS and a Bubblewrap `--ro-bind` on Linux, so none of them can plant a hook, change `.git/config` or move a ref.
  A `core.hooksPath` that names a directory outside the git directory (a repository that uses husky keeps its hooks in `.husky/_`) is denied to the layer the same way, and an opted-out run digests it.
  A `core.hooksPath` directory that does not exist yet resolves to the real path of its nearest existing ancestor plus the components below it: Seatbelt denies that path, and Bubblewrap, which cannot bind an absent path (the run creates no directory in your tree), leaves it to the reading, which holds whether the directory exists, so a hooks directory created during the run ends it with exit 12.
  When the checkout is a linked worktree or a submodule, its `.git` is a file outside the common git directory: the layer is denied a write to it (a Seatbelt `(literal ...)` deny rule, a Bubblewrap `--ro-bind` of the file), and the reading holds the git directory the checkout resolves to and the content of the file, so a `.git` file rewritten to point into a copy of the repository ends the run with exit 12 even when a write path was missed.
  The reading's `git status` runs with `core.fsmonitor` switched off, so a checkout redirected to a repository the layer controls does not run that repository's fsmonitor command from the runtime's own read.
  `selectConfinement` takes the project root as a required argument.
  A project in no git repository has no such directory.
  An opted-out run (`"confinement": false`) keeps comparing the refs, the shared git state and the hooks directory, since its target can write them: a target that runs `git update-ref`, writes `.git/config` or writes a hook still exits 12.
  The CLI reference tells a maintainer who shares a repository with other sessions to run an opted-out evaluation from a standalone clone.
  `test:evaluate-confinement` runs a held confined run through a commit, tag, gc, `push -u` and `worktree add -b` in a second worktree, a conflicting rebase stopped in the main checkout of a project that is a linked worktree, a tracked edit, an untracked file and a commit in the project's own checkout, a confined evaluator whose `git update-ref`, `.git/config` write, hook write and write into the `core.hooksPath` directory are refused (with the evaluation folder inside and outside the project's repository, and the same evaluator opted out, whose writes land and stop the run), a confined evaluator whose write to the `.git` file of a linked-worktree project is refused (and lands, stopping the run, when opted out), a confined evaluator whose creation of an absent `core.hooksPath` directory is refused on Seatbelt and ends the run or is refused on Bubblewrap, a gitfile redirected to a copy of the repository that moves the confined reading, a confined target that runs `git update-ref` and `git config`, the opted-out writes of a ref, the configuration and a hook, the Seatbelt profile and the Bubblewrap argument list of the layer, and the reference sentences under their heading.
  The cases run on the host's own mechanism, so macOS runs them under Seatbelt and the ubuntu CI job runs them under Bubblewrap.
- The Windows helper-death case of `test:evaluate-preflight` no longer fails when Windows hands the case's guardian a reused PID. Windows keeps a dead parent's PID in each orphan's `ParentProcessId`, so a guardian that received such a PID listed a stranger beside its agent and Job Object helper, the case could not single out the helper, never killed it, and reported four failures (the discovery check, a 15 s runner wait and both 10 s survival checks). The case's child lookup now keeps only processes created no earlier than the current holder of the parent PID, and its discovery line prints the guardian's children so a mismatch shows what it found.
- A confined run over a project that uses sparse checkout now shows the target the project's status (Story 1.85, AD-7, AD-8). Before, the private repository's index was rebuilt with a plain `git read-tree HEAD`, which sets no skip-worktree bit, so the target's `git status` listed every tracked file outside the cone as deleted where the project's status listed nothing. This held for a cone, a pattern list and a blob-less clone made with `--sparse`. The private repository now carries the worktree's `core.sparseCheckout`, `core.sparseCheckoutCone` and, when the worktree keeps a sparse index, `index.sparse`, the worktree keeps its own patterns, and `git read-tree -m -u HEAD` marks the files outside the cone skip-worktree, so `git status` lists no deletion, `git ls-files` lists the files outside the cone and `git sparse-checkout list` prints the project's patterns, and the long form of `git status` reports the sparse checkout (a sparse index included) as the project's does. A project that is not sparse keeps the index it has. A failing `git read-tree -m -u HEAD` refuses the run (exit 12) so the target is never handed false deletions. The runtime also removes the `config.worktree` that `git worktree add` copies into the worktree's metadata directory, which a target could read and which can hold the worktree-scoped remotes, URLs and credential helpers of a project that set any. The `probe-sparse` stub prints each line, and the case compares it with the project's own for a cone, a pattern list, a cone with a sparse index and a `--sparse` blob-less clone, and the reference's `### File-system confinement` states it.
- `test:probe-targets` no longer fails when a file in the checkout changes while a harness case runs (Story 1.99). The behavioral harnesses read every file that becomes modified or untracked anywhere in the checkout during a run as a write by the run and answer exit 2 with a lost run, so an editor, a formatter or another suite touching the checkout in that window failed two to four checks (the atdd `load-error` case, the ci `unparseable` case) and then passed on the next run. The suite now runs a case again, in a fresh copy of the checkout at HEAD that no writer from outside can reach, when a file in the checkout changes while it runs, so a brief outside write no longer fails it. A write the stub makes happens again in the copy and fails the case, whether it is a new file, a write into a tracked file, a deletion, a write that happens only when a file is missing or one that happens only when a directory was just created, which a repeat in place hid behind the first attempt's leftover. The copy is paid only when a case reports the change. A stand-in harness over a scratch repository plants each of those beside a save made once from outside. Reproduced by creating untracked files in a copy of the checkout while the suite ran: every harness case failed before the change, and none failed after it when the writes lasted a few seconds.
- A confined run now accepts a project cloned with a promisor remote, keeps its tags and has no history size limit (Story 1.80, AD-7, AD-8). Before, a confined run over a `--filter=blob:none` or `--filter=tree:0` clone exited 12 because packing the history would fetch all of it, the target's git carried no tag, so `git describe --tags` and a build that reads a tag found none, and a history of more than about six million objects exited 12 when the object walk's output exceeded the buffer the runtime read it into. Now the private repository holds the objects the clone holds on disk and no process of a confined run fetches from the remote (git 2.44 or later; an older git exits 12 with the way out named, and so does a historical probe's checkout of a revision the clone lacks on disk, which names the revision), it carries the project's tags that point into the evaluated history (lightweight and annotated, with no remote, URL, credential or hook), and the walks that grow with the history stream through a reader that keeps one line in memory. A filter driver whose name holds a space, a `required` written with no value and a `core.` setting written with no value now reach the private repository, so the target's git configuration matches the project's. The reference's `### File-system confinement` drops the three limits.
- Escaped echoes of injected HTTP credentials now scrub when individual Unicode letters change case unevenly before a serializer writes `\uXXXX` (Story 1.74). The observation's headers, body, nested values and keys, plus fault messages, causes and cut captured text, use the same matching at one or two JSON escape levels. Greek final sigma, Turkish dotted `İ`, Cyrillic letters and astral surrogate pairs are covered.
- A preflight now reclaims a verified dead run's private parent and its auxiliary scratch, including engine staging, command evaluator working directories and Linux Bubblewrap status directories, after `SIGKILL` and across temp-directory changes on POSIX and Windows. Durable journal and parent markers bind recovery to the same evaluation and invocation. An interrupted marker write is recoverable when its bytes match the record's prefix and the parent contains nothing else. Handled signals retire cleaned records; normal cleanup and recovery remove every direct child before the parent marker, so an interrupted removal can resume on a later preflight. Bubblewrap status uses a host-held secret and an authenticated final record, so a target cannot forge its start or signal status. Live, linked and unverifiable parents remain untouched (Story 1.54).
- The CI quality chain now uses 12 shards and refreshed measured weights. After the eval-quality 6.0.1 update, the eight-shard run completed every chain 4 script with zero failures but reached the 20-minute job cap before coverage upload; the wider split gives the long agent suite its own runner (Story 1.73).
- The learned Autoevals pantry fixture now scores `PASS` on published eval-quality 6.0.1 for all three development probes and the held-out probe. Its scalar CLI oracle checks exit code 0 and exact whole stdout together; the critical `success-indicator-separation` gap closes without changing the contract or its three-trial clean and defect outcomes. TeA's optional peer floor, publish guard, release metadata gate, CLI prerequisite and missing-engine guidance now require 6.0.1. Three accepted CI fixture baselines were reaccepted with 6.0.1 while preserving their verdicts and evidence (Story 1.55).
- The CI chain shards use refreshed evaluator timings and a 20-minute job bound. Story 1.72's evaluator suite took 491 seconds under coverage, twice its old shard weight; the old 15-minute job cap canceled shard 8 during its final markdown lint script after all tests had passed.
- On POSIX, a detached watchdog now ends an agent's process group if its guardian is stopped and the leader and supervisor die together (Story 1.53). The guardian waits for watchdog readiness before launching the agent; setup failure leaves the agent unstarted.
- Windows agent supervision now establishes a kill-on-close Job Object before launching the agent. Agent descendants end after a normal agent exit or a combined leader and supervisor kill, and setup failures stop the launch. The guardian launches PowerShell from the host's absolute SystemRoot path, preventing a project-local executable from claiming Job Object readiness. PowerShell setup has a 90 s bound and a 105 s supervisor startup backstop; the agent's `--timeout-ms` clock begins after its PID is confirmed. A dedicated, bounded report pipe carries Windows guardian readiness and completion. Windows CI probes both guardian startup and the full runner before the descendant matrix (Story 1.52).
- The macOS confinement audit mechanism gate retries a missing kernel report after its scenario retries are exhausted. A report loss during a busy test run no longer fails the full gate when a later attempt observes the expected read.
- The baseline comparison test now asks Git to copy fixture repositories before overlaying untracked run evidence. This avoids a macOS Node crash when a loose-object directory disappears during a native `fs.cpSync` traversal. The copy preserves a detached source HEAD, working-tree deletions and nested Git metadata.
- A Bubblewrap target has no route to the host's abstract Unix sockets (Story 1.63, AD-8).
  Every process `tea-evaluate` starts for a Linux target whose entry keeps the default network now runs in a network namespace of its own (`--unshare-net`): a loopback and nothing else, so a desktop session's D-Bus and every other abstract socket of the host do not exist for it.
  An HTTP server such a target starts stays reachable through a bridge the runtime owns, with no network path between the namespaces: the status shim serves a Unix socket in a private directory of the call, readiness is the bridge's answer for the address and port the server bound, and the runtime listens on the address and port the call is configured for and forwards each connection through the socket.
  A server that reports its port reports the one it bound inside the namespace, and the runtime listens on the same number when the host has it free and on a port the system gives otherwise; a chosen port is the runtime's from before the server starts.
  The runtime connects to the bridge's socket through a descriptor that follows no link and checks that it is a socket of its own user, so a target that writes the directory cannot steer the connection to another socket, and a temp directory too long for a socket path puts the directory under `/tmp`.
  A host that cannot create the namespace is refused at selection (exit 12) as one that cannot start Bubblewrap is.
  macOS Seatbelt is unchanged, and path-based sockets the read-only `/` shows (`/var/run/docker.sock`, the system bus) stay connectable until Story 1.82.
  `test:evaluate-run` drives the bridge protocol, its refusals, its streams and the shim's endings over Unix sockets on every host, `test:evaluate-api` drives the forwarder and the readiness semantics through the real HTTP port and a stood-in namespace, and the Linux CI job proves the abstract socket is refused and that the same commands reach it once `--unshare-net` is taken out.
  The reference's confinement section states the closed route, and each sentence of it that speaks of the network names the case that backs it.
- `tea-evaluate run` scores the bytes of an evaluator qualification attempt that its runtime wrote (Story 1.69).
  `run` qualifies a sealed-brief agent evaluator by scoring each attempt through `eval-quality score` over files it wrote into the run directory a moment before.
  In a run that opted out of file-system confinement, a target's leftover process could rewrite the attempt's record, the contract, the policy, the probe, the preflight verdict, the manifest or the evaluator configuration, or substitute a well-formed artifact with altered votes for the staged one.
  The votes that decide whether the evaluator qualifies then came from bytes the runtime never wrote.
  `run` now reads those seven inputs once through the run directory writer, which hands back only the bytes the runtime wrote, and an input that is not those bytes exits 12 naming the file before any call.
  After every attempt's call it reads each input again, names the first that changed, and requires the call to match an in-process score of the held bytes: the staged artifact byte for byte, and the call's exit and its `eval-quality:` lines (an Invalid attempt's included).
  Any failure exits 12 naming the file or the mismatch, records no vote for the call and writes no `evaluator-qualification.json`.
  The staged bytes the comparison accepted are the ones copied in and read for the vote.
  The comparison only refuses, and the recorded argv of each attempt, run by hand with a fresh `--out`, still reproduces its evidence byte for byte.
  The comparison moved from `score.js` to `cli/lib/evaluate/held-refusal.js` so `score` and `run` share it and its wording, and `score`'s behavior and text are unchanged.
  `test:evaluate-boundaries` follows it there and refuses a re-score asked for in `score.js` or `run.js`.
  The new `test:evaluate-held-attempts` script (`--group=held-attempts` of `test/test-evaluate-evaluators.js`, in the `npm test` chain of 109 steps) rewrites each input for the real engine's read, kept on a later attempt and a later probe and restored on a later attempt.
  It also substitutes, reformats, links, removes and restages artifacts and exits, and a static read fails when `scoreAttempt` names an input without `score-inputs.js`.
  The reference's `Score input integrity` and `Qualifying a sealed-brief agent` sections state the check.
- The test-design contract reports whole-document coverage (Story 1.48, eval-quality's AD-20 rule 2 and AD-31).
  eval-quality's `whole-body` rule is satisfied when one oracle's direction and check both address every required response key of an operation at one step, and a parent pointer does not address a key.
  The test-design operation declares four, `design`, `riskRowCount`, `scoredRiskDescriptions` and `scoredRiskCount`, and every oracle read a subset, so the baseline reported a gap although the material oracles read the complete Markdown.
  The engine's reading is right, so the fix is in the contract: `tools/generate-contracts.js` appends one `projection-coherence` oracle per fixture set (`O-016` and `O-017`, after the existing fifteen, whose numbers hold) that names all four key pointers and the design artifact in its direction and its check.
  The oracle reads every key and fails only for a defect in the runner's projection. The workflow's Markdown is still judged by the material oracles.
  The check states that the projection has exactly its four declared keys, each of its declared type, that `design` equals the design artifact the run wrote and has a non-blank character, that `scoredRiskCount` is zero exactly when `scoredRiskDescriptions` is empty and that `riskRowCount` is not zero whenever `scoredRiskCount` is not.
  The vocabulary has no ordering between two evidence values and no pointer to a collection's length, so exact count equality and `riskRowCount >= scoredRiskCount` need an enumeration over the declared cardinality bound, which costs 60 to 68 KB of JSON per oracle and was judged too large.
  `test:contract-oracles` asserts those two relations and the equality of `design` and the artifact directly on every stored projection, plants incoherent projections, one per claim, and compares the oracle with its harness-side scorer.
  It also scores the zero-action probe through eval-quality against the real contract, against the contract without the oracles and against versions where the oracle reads only the scored descriptions, exactly three keys or names one key fewer in its direction, so the engine's own coverage function holds the criterion.
  `test/probes/expected-strength.json` drops `whole-body` from the test-design coverage gaps, and every probe verdict and exit code is unchanged.
  The oracles carry no defect probe because no document makes the projection incoherent, and the gameability probe's signature leaves them out because a signature cannot address the design artifact.
  The routing, test-review and trace contracts close the same gap in Story 1.100.
  No eval-quality change and no release.
- Evaluate's guidance for the `whole-body` coverage gap names the repair that closes it (Story 1.48).
  `references/gaps.md` and `references/contract.md` told the author to compare the full response body, but the rule is satisfied only when one oracle names every required response key pointer in both its direction and its check, and a parent or body-root pointer never addresses a key.
  The `whole-body` row now says that, and the `### whole-body` section teaches a multi-key response with one oracle naming each key pointer, which `test:evaluate-guidance` compiles through the published engine.
- `test/test-contracts.js` and `test/contracts/expected-status.json` carry the same note again: the writer said "locked" and the committed file said "lockfile-resolved", so `--write` changed a file nothing else had touched.
- `test/probes/README.md` states that the test-design probe corpus is scored (Story 1.48). `test:probe-corpus` has scored it since its evidence source was wired, the README said it was unscored, and it now records why the projection oracles have no probe.
- The test-design probe corpus claims `rollbackVerified: true` only for a mutation it qualified (Story 1.49, AD-8).
  Before, `tools/generate-probes.js` wrote the claim as a constant for each test-design controlled-mutation probe because the reference design and the seeded design sit side by side on disk, which proves no rollback.
  Now each of the fourteen probes is qualified in a disposable workspace through the runtime's `runMutationCycle`: the clean arm scores a copy of the reference design, the one exact `replace-exact` edit that yields the stored seeded design is applied, the mutated arm scores it, the original bytes are restored and their digest compared, and the clean arm scores again.
  Each arm is `projectTestDesignResult`, the projection `test:eval-replay` stores (moved to `test/lib/test-design-result.js`), and its result must equal the one the stored run records.
  A failed restore, a mismatched digest, a clean arm that fails, a mutated arm that holds, a clean rerun that fails, a stored result the cycle did not reproduce or a stored design that changed during the cycle stops the generator before any probe is written.
  The generated probes and `expected-strength.json` are unchanged byte for byte, and the staged test-design preflight keeps every expected outcome.
  Each cycle's workspace is a directory under the runtime's private root, outside the checkout whatever `TMPDIR` is, and the end of the cycle removes it. A SIGINT or SIGTERM ends the generator by its default action, and the next cycle reclaims the dead process's parent.
  `npm run test:test-design-qualification` joins the `npm test` chain and plants each failure.
  The test-review, trace, nfr and ci corpora keep their stored-pair claim until Story 1.99.
- The test-design evaluation keeps a labeled reference table out of the scored register (Story 1.47). A table under a heading whose last word is Example(s), Illustration(s) or Reference(s) (including a heading that is the single word Reference), or whose first word is Example(s) or Illustration(s) (after an optional Worked) followed by a colon or a spaced dash, optionally after one number or letter, or by Register or Table, optionally after Risk, or under a paragraph that opens `Example:`, `Worked example:` or `Illustration:` (which labels every table up to the next heading or the end of its list item), joins no register count, unsupported-risk oracle, risk precision figure or coverage mapping. The document's first heading is its title and labels nothing, and citations such as a `Reference:` or `References:` line (a `## References` heading labels its tables), `For example:` and domain names such as `Sample Intake Risks` or `Reference Data Risks` label nothing. Before, any unfenced table with risk ID and score columns was the register. A run that leaves tables out prints each excluded label and row count and records them as summary evidence in its diagnostics. The runner projection and the harness read one parser, so the contract, projection and scorer agree. The parser documents the rule against the shipped worked example, and a stored design pairs a reference table and a scored row that carry the same ruled-out category. The replay counts in the eval-quality adoption guide and roadmap are now held by `doc-counts`, so a new stored case cannot leave them stale.
- `tea-evaluate` scrubs an injected value from an observation in whatever letter case the target echoes it (Story 1.66, AD-4, AD-8).
  Before, an observation matched the value only in the case the host knew it, so a header a server normalizes, a field it shouts or a URL a proxy lowercases left the value in the evidence artifacts, and through the release a deployment reports, in a refusal's quoted identifier in `refused/<probeId>.json` and `run.json`.
  Now the observation, a fault's message and cause, a cut text's leading part (four characters or more) and a number's text all compare case-folded text with the case-folded forms of the value, built once per set of values: lower, upper, capitalized per word and mixed cases are replaced, as are the mappings that change a text's length (`ß` and `SS`, `İ`) and the Turkish `i` and `I`.
  Secrets that overlap in a text are replaced as one span, and a large value (a credential file or a certificate chain) scrubs without a size limit.
  The fault path no longer has a lowercased set of its own.
  A value shorter than eight characters stays unscrubbed in every case.
- `tea-evaluate run` refuses the records of a `records` evaluator that were sealed against another configuration (Story 1.67). Each sealed record and isolation manifest names `evaluatorConfigurationDigest`, the digest of the harness's final `evaluator-configuration.json`, and `score` refuses a mismatch with exit 3, but `check` and `run` exited 0 first, so a harness that sealed its records and then bound its calibration values only learned of it at `score`. `run` now holds every imported record and isolation manifest to the imported configuration's digest (the one it records in `run.json`), exits 10 with the file and both digests, and copies nothing; `check` reads no imported record and stays as it was. `run` also exits 10, not 12, for a configuration that meets the schema but that eval-quality cannot digest (a number such as `1e21`, a lone surrogate), naming `evaluator-configuration.json`.
- A promptfoo result that the framework could not grade no longer counts as a caught target defect (Story 1.43). The promptfoo fixture evaluator and Evaluate's `promptfoo-assertions.mjs` starter turned a result with no `gradingResult` and an `error` string into a failing judgment row that cited the target's stdout, so an evaluation failure scored like a violated oracle. Both evaluators now refuse a result whose `gradingResult` is absent or null, with or without an `error`, naming the ungraded framework error; `tea-evaluate run` ends as evaluator infrastructure failure (exit 12) and seals no record for that trial. A graded `pass: false` still becomes a `fail` row that quotes the observed stdout, a graded `pass: true` still becomes `pass`, and a graded failure without a reason carries `Assertion failed.`. The imported result must carry every expected assertion grade exactly once, so a partial, repeated or malformed grade set stops the trial with no record even when another assertion graded. The evaluator guide gains a `## Separate ungraded framework errors from graded target failures` section with two runnable examples that `test:evaluate-guidance` runs through the rendered starter, and the Story 1.20 rule that turned an ungraded result into a failing row is marked superseded in the plan. With promptfoo 0.123.1 a thrown `javascript` assertion or a crashing code file arrives graded `pass: false`, which the next entry (Story 1.70) refuses by assertion type and value; an ungraded row arises when promptfoo cannot grade at all, for example for a `contains` assertion with no usable `value`.
- A promptfoo assertion that runs your code or calls a model no longer counts as a caught target defect (Story 1.70, AD-10, AD-21). With promptfoo 0.123.1 a `javascript` assertion that throws, and a `contains` whose `value` is a `file://` reference to a Python or Ruby file that raises, arrive as a graded `pass: false` whose `reason` is the crash text, and promptfoo sets `error` on every failed assertion, so the crash scored like a violated oracle. A `transform` rewrote the output before the assertion graded it, so the row graded text the target never produced. The promptfoo fixture evaluator and Evaluate's `promptfoo-assertions.mjs` starter now admit only `contains`, `icontains`, `contains-all`, `contains-any`, `icontains-all`, `icontains-any`, `equals`, `starts-with`, `regex` and `is-json`, each also with a `not-` prefix. Any other type (`javascript`, `python`, `ruby`, `webhook`, a model-graded type), an admitted type whose `value` is a `file://` reference to a `.js`, `.cjs`, `.mjs`, `.ts`, `.cts`, `.mts`, `.py` or `.rb` file (as the value or as an element of an array value, in any letter case) or a string `value` that starts with `package:`, a string `value` or array element that contains `{{`, `{%` or `{#` (promptfoo renders it as a template, which can run code and can derive the expected value from the output), a `regex` or `not-regex` pattern that does not compile or that is a `file://` reference (promptfoo compiles the file's content after the wrapper's check), a `weight` of `0` (promptfoo reports a failed assertion with that weight as a pass), and any `transform` other than `null`, stops the trial with a diagnostic naming the type and the reason; `tea-evaluate run` ends as evaluator infrastructure failure (exit 12) and seals no record. A `file://` reference to a data file (`.json`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.txt`) and a `package:` string inside an array value stay admitted. The wrapper reads the assertions promptfoo reports it ran, so promptfoo has already run the code when it refuses; an assertion that needs code belongs in a `command` evaluator you own, where a crash exits non-zero. The evaluator guide's `## Separate ungraded framework errors from graded target failures` section teaches the allow-list, the value guard, `transform` and that route with a runnable refused example, and `test:evaluate-promptfoo` and `test:evaluate-guidance` run each refusal through the fixture evaluator and the rendered starter, and the `javascript`, `file://boom.py`, `transform`, template, `weight: 0` and invalid `regex` cases through promptfoo itself.
- The chain shards no longer run uneven (Story 1.43). Script weights in `tools/test-shard-weights.json` had drifted from the CI measures under coverage, so on one run chain shard 1 took 14m24 of its 15 minute cap while shard 2 took 8 minutes, and a later run of shard 1 hit the cap. Twenty-seven weights now follow the times one CI run measured for every script, which brings the five shards to within a second of each other by the weights (about 682 seconds each, from 467 to 773 in the measured run). No timeout moved.
- A confined target can no longer read the evaluation folder from your project's git history (Story 1.57). Before, a target that ran `git show <commit>:<evaluation folder>/contract.json`, or read a blob it found through `git ls-tree`, got the committed contract, probes and mutations, because its worktree read your repository's objects. Now each git workspace of a confined run reads a private repository built from the evaluated commit's full history, with the evaluation folder as an empty tree at every commit. Commit ids, `commitDigest` and `implementationDigest` are unchanged, and the target's `git status`, `git log`, `git diff` and `git show` over the rest of the tree work as before. Your project's git directory, and any object store it borrows from, is withheld from the target under Seatbelt and Bubblewrap, apart from the worktree's own entry; the audit reports a Node process that opens it. The first workspace of a run packs your history, so a large project pays that time once at the start, and each later workspace for the same commit links the same objects. A project that is a partial clone is refused with exit 12 in a confined run, since packing it would fetch all of its history. A run with `"confinement": false` keeps today's worktree and builds nothing. The confinement cases of `test:evaluate-run` now run as their own script, `test:evaluate-confinement`, in the `npm test` chain. The reference's `### File-system confinement` states these limits, and `test:evaluate-run` fails while the old sentence saying the history stays readable returns.
- `test:evaluate-run` and `test:evaluate-evaluators` no longer carry their whole file's wall time (Story 1.68). In CI under coverage `test:evaluate-run` measured 360 seconds and `test:evaluate-evaluators` 480.5, past the 400-second line. `test:evaluate-run` keeps the run, score and refusal cases, `test:evaluate-confinement` takes the confinement cases, `test:evaluate-aggregate` the strength aggregate cases and `test:evaluate-held-inputs` the held-input cases (`--group=run`, `--group=confinement`, `--group=aggregate` and `--group=held-inputs` of `test/test-evaluate-run.js`), and `test:evaluate-agents` takes the sealed-brief agent cases of `test/test-evaluate-evaluators.js` (`--group=agents`). All join the `npm test` chain, whose count in the README reads 103 and is written in digits; `tools/test-shard-weights.json` carries a weight for each.
- `tea-evaluate score` scores the bytes it verified (Story 1.68). `score` checked every input against the digests `run.json` recorded and then handed eval-quality their paths, so in a run that opted out of file-system confinement a target's leftover process could rewrite a record, the contract, the policy, a probe or a manifest between the check and the engine's read and have the rewritten bytes scored, or substitute a well-formed artifact with altered outcomes for the staged one. `score` now reads each input once without following a link, digests it with eval-quality's `digestBytes`, compares the digest with `run.json` and keeps the bytes. After every `eval-quality score` call it reads and digests every input again and names the first file that changed or appeared (a manifest absent at the check and present afterwards counts), then scores the held bytes in process with eval-quality's library and requires the call to match: the staged artifact must equal the serialized result byte for byte (a call that stages nothing must match a result with no artifact, whatever it exited), and the call's exit and its `eval-quality:` diagnostic lines must be the ones the held bytes give (when the library refuses the held bytes, only the exit is compared). Any failure exits 12 naming the file or the mismatch and copies no evidence for that call. The run-wide strength aggregate call (Story 1.45) is held to the same inputs: after it `score` re-reads every input, the run's policy included, reads the persisted evidence back and compares it with the bytes it copied, aggregates the held bytes in process and requires the staged aggregate to equal the result byte for byte and the call's exit to be the one the held bytes give (so an aggregate call that exits 64 exits 12); a policy rewritten for the aggregate and kept or restored, an unreadable policy, and a well-formed substituted aggregate exit 12 with no aggregate copied. The re-score only compares and refuses; the eval-quality CLI still decides every verdict and exit, and the recorded argv still names the run directory's own files, so rerunning it with a fresh `--out` gives byte-identical evidence. `partitions.json`, `gap-view.json` and `interpretation.json` are built from the held bytes too. `test:evaluate-boundaries` lets `cli/lib/evaluate/score-inputs.js` alone name `runScore`. `test:evaluate-partitions` rewrites each of the seven inputs for the real engine's read and keeps the rewrite, `test:evaluate-run` restores it, plants a manifest and substitutes a schema-valid artifact, and the reference's `Score input integrity` section replaces its sentence that a process able to write the run directory could rewrite a file and its digest.
- `tea-evaluate score` keeps every score output inside the run directory while a process changes it (Story 1.41). Story 1.21 refused a link planted at `scores` before scoring; a link swapped in for `scores`, an invocation directory or a probe directory while `score` ran could still carry a write into your working tree. `score` now attaches to the run directory the way `run` holds its own: it adopts a real `scores` directory or creates it, creates each invocation and probe directory exclusively, and writes `score.json`, the evidence artifact, `partitions.json`, `gap-view.json` and `interpretation.json` through the held directories. A link or other entry at a score directory or output file, or a directory replaced or moved during scoring, exits 12 naming the entry before any byte leaves the run directory, and so does a link at `runs/`, which `run` already refused. eval-quality's `--out` is now a private staging file outside the evaluation folder, removed after each call; the artifact is copied in as `evidence-artifact.json` only when it meets its published schema, names the run's corpus digest and carries an outcome for the probe (Story 1.68, below, also checks it against an in-process score of the verified inputs), and `score.json` keeps the argv exactly as it ran, so rerunning `eval-quality score` on it with a fresh `--out` gives byte-identical evidence. A repeated `score` still keeps every earlier invocation. `test:evaluate-partitions` races link swaps and plants against the real engine and compares the adopter repository's git status and external sentinels, and `test:evaluate-run` holds the writer, the staged-artifact checks and the reference's `Score output integrity` section.
- A confined target can no longer read the bridge's admission token or the run's private working directories (Story 1.58). Before, the configuration file that carries the sealed-brief agent bridge's one admission token, the bridge's socket, and the working directories of an evaluator, the judge and a `command` evaluator were directories under the temp directory, so a confined target running as your user could read the token before the agent connected and take the bridge's one admission, or read an evaluator's or judge's working files. Now every run of your user makes one private parent directory when it starts, before any sandbox is built, beneath one private root, `/tmp/tea-evaluate-p<uid>` (mode 700, a directory you own that is not a link), whatever the run's `TMPDIR` is, so every run of your user shares the one root a sandbox withholds. Every directory the evaluation layer makes for itself (the bridge's configuration, token file and socket, the engine's, the qualification's and `score`'s staging directories, and the evaluator, judge and `command` evaluator working directories) is made beneath the parent. (`compare --accept` stages the new baseline inside the evaluation folder, which is already withheld, so it keeps its place.) The confinement withholds the root from every target and every process it leaves running, so a leftover process started before a directory existed, a process an earlier run left running and a target of a concurrent run all stay out of this run's directories: Seatbelt denies each read and write under the root and each connection to a unix socket under it, Bubblewrap covers it with an empty read-only file system, and the audit reports a Node process that opens it. The bridge's token now exists only in a mode 600 file beneath the parent, and the relay's environment and argument list carry that file's path (`TEA_EVALUATE_BRIDGE_TOKEN_FILE` replaces `TEA_EVALUATE_BRIDGE_TOKEN`), since another process of your user, a confined target included, can read a process's environment. The agent's own connection and every process of the evaluation layer are admitted as before. The directories a target is granted (its workspace, its temp directory, the audit report, the status and port files) stay where they were. A run removes its own parent however it ends apart from SIGKILL, which leaves the parent in place until Story 1.54 reclaims it; the shared root stays, since another run may be using it. Evidence, records and digests are unchanged, and a run with `"confinement": false` keeps today's layout. The reference's `#### The bridge's admission token` states the withholding, and `test:evaluate-confinement` fails while the old sentence saying a target can read the token returns. The cases that run confined sealed-brief agents, signal-ended runs and the private parent run as their own script, `test:evaluate-private`, in the `npm test` chain, so no chain script carries their wall time.
- The two tests that read `npm pack --dry-run --json` (`test:install`'s builder-artifact exclusion and `test:layering-boundary-lineage`'s published-file scan) read both shapes npm prints: an array with one entry per package (npm 11 and earlier) and one object keyed by the package name (npm 12). The publish workflow installs the latest npm, so with npm 12.2.0 `test:install` failed there on `object is not iterable` and every push to `main` still failed at `Run tests` after the lockfile fix. `test/lib/pack-listing.js` holds the reading, with a unit for each shape.
- The publish workflow installs the latest npm on the runner. Its pinned `npm@11.6.2` could not `npm ci` a lockfile written by a newer npm (`Missing: google-logging-utils@1.2.0 from lock file`), so every push to `main` since the lockfile moved failed to publish.
- Evaluate's gap guide now states the engine's `malformed-input` contract requirement: every relevant operation needs a type-violating planned input and an oracle check addressing its step. A blind repair exposed that caught malformed probes alone left the coverage gap open (Story 1.25).
- The pre-commit gate clears Git's hook-local repository selectors while running `npm test`, and the test-review CLI fixture clears inherited Git selectors before building its scratch repository. This keeps nested fixture checkouts from changing the contributor's branch (Story 1.24).
- The Evaluate HTTP probe port enforces its elapsed cap across resolution, server preparation, sending and redirects, including large declared caps. Typed mutations reject string-only CLI and header bindings before launch (Story 1.24).
- A guardian stops an agent's process group after a simultaneous supervisor and leader kill. Evaluate records each workspace in a held private journal, keeps an ownership marker through interrupted cleanup, supervises Git checkout and removal, and reclaims verified scratch and detached Git registrations from killed runs on the next preflight (Story 1.28).
- The README's suite counts and `test:cli`'s `eval:all` invocation count read only the manifest suites a harness runs, so an `evaluate-authored` entry, which declares no runner capabilities and which `eval:all` skips, no longer crashes `test:doc-count-sources` or miscounts the live harnesses (Story 1.16).
- The test-design evaluation treats ruled-out categories in risk rows scored 1–3 as documented guards. Rows scored above 3 trigger invented-risk oracles and probes. The runner derives a scored-risk view from the harness parser. Test-design risk tables now use the direct `markdown-it` dependency, so reordered columns, lists, fences and indented code resolve through Markdown tokens (Story 1.27).
- The Evaluate interpretation fixture clears inherited Git repository variables before creating its scratch repository, so the full test gate can run safely inside a Git commit hook.
- The mutation test clears its interrupted-run deadline when the child closes, so `npm run test:evaluate-mutation` exits promptly after reporting success.
- `tea-evaluate score` refuses a planted `scores` symlink before running the scorer, so a pre-existing link cannot redirect score artifacts into the adopter's repository. The remaining concurrent directory-swap case is tracked by Story 1.41.
- A `copy` workspace reproduced for a trial or a qualification now holds what the pristine copy held when it was made (Story 1.18).
  The reproduction copied the pristine copy as it stood after the preflight legs ran in it, so a target whose operation writes (a workflow step that stores a record) left the pristine copy changed, and every later workspace was refused with exit 12 as not reproducing it; the pristine copy now keeps a snapshot beside itself, its links contained and its provisioned directories left out, and a snapshot changed after it was made still exits 12.
  A reproduction provisions only the directories the pristine copy provisioned when it was made, so a provisioned directory a target makes there during the legs reaches no later workspace, and one planted in the snapshot or moved out of the pristine copy exits 12.
  The snapshot digest now includes empty directories and permission bits, and a reproduction refuses a provisioned copy whose bytes or permissions changed after creation, so later trials cannot inherit a target's edits to supposedly read-only inputs.
- A gameability arm's command step reads its committed degenerate response through eval-quality's command-line adapter, as a sealed-brief agent's gameability calls already did (Story 1.18).
  The plan's steps had recorded a command's degenerate output as text whatever it held, where a real run and the agent's calls read JSON-shaped output as JSON, so an oracle or a captured binding pointing into it resolved nothing; a plan step on a gameability arm is also held to the registry's authorizations now, as the agent's calls were, and a step the registry denies exits 10, as on a real arm; no host value is injected into or scrubbed from a plan step's committed answer, so it reads the same on every host.
  A committed gameability probe whose degenerate response prints JSON-shaped command output is now judged over JSON: re-run its qualification, since an oracle that read that output as text can resolve otherwise.

- `.gitignore` ignored most of the files this repository tracks under `_bmad-output/` (the Evaluate plan, its stories and three earlier planning files), so Prettier, which reads `.gitignore`, formatted none of them in lint-staged or `npm run format:check`, and every commit touching one printed lint-staged's "[FAILED] The following paths are ignored" from its re-add.
  `.gitignore` names each tracked file back in, the 24 files Prettier had skipped are formatted, `.prettierignore` names the append-only `.memlog.md` as `.markdownlint-cli2.yaml` already did, and `npm run test:bmad-output-gated` fails when `git check-ignore` reads a tracked `_bmad-output/` file as ignored or Prettier skips one `.prettierignore` does not name.
- `llms.txt` linked every documentation page under a `/docs/` prefix the site does not serve, so each of its page links was dead; it now links each page at the path the site serves, and `npm run docs:build` fails on a page link that names no document under `docs/`.
- `llms.txt` and `llms-full.txt` linked `https://test-architect.bmad-method.org`, a domain with no DNS record, whenever the build ran without `SITE_URL`, which the published site does. `npm run docs:build` now resolves the site URL through `website/src/lib/site-url.js`, the resolver the Astro site uses, so both files link the site that was built; `test:direction` scans that folder as its own layer, which only `tools/` may import and which imports no package. The issue template's troubleshooting link, `robots.txt` and `website/README.md` named the same domain and now name the published site.
- `tea-test-review` no longer holds its stderr open for up to 15 s after it exits, and its progress heartbeat no longer outlives a CLI that was killed.
  The heartbeat was an `sh` loop around `sleep`, and stopping it left the running `sleep` holding the stderr it inherited, so a caller reading that stderr through a pipe (`| tee`, a CI step capturing output, a script's `spawnSync`) waited for end of file long after the review finished.
  It is now `cli/lib/heartbeat.js`, a single Node process that starts nothing of its own, so stopping it closes its stderr at once; it also exits within 100 ms once the CLI is gone, so a CLI killed by `SIGKILL` leaves no heartbeat printing into its stderr.
  The CLI's own test file drove 35 stub-agent reviews through that wait: its Suite 7 went from about 9.4 minutes in CI to about 6 s locally, and the whole file runs in about 14 s.
- The Evaluate test suites (`test:evaluate-check`, `test:evaluate-preflight`, `test:evaluate-mutation` and `test:evaluate-run`) left their temp directories in the system temp directory whenever a signal stopped them.
  Each suite now makes its directories under one parent named for its process and removes it when it ends, and the next run of the suite removes a parent whose process is gone; `test:evaluate-run` also asserts that every run and score leaves its project's temp directory empty.
- `tea-skill-runner` reports the signal that asked an agent's process group to stop beside how the agent ended: an agent that outlived the grace period after a forwarded or received `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP` or `SIGQUIT` reads as killed by `SIGKILL` once it outlived the grace period after that signal, where the report named the `SIGKILL` alone.
  A `SIGQUIT` writes a core file by default, and on a system that hands cores to a collector (a Linux CI runner) the dump can outlast the 2 s grace period, which made `test:evaluate-preflight`'s forwarded-`SIGQUIT` case fail intermittently; that case now accepts either ending, both naming `SIGQUIT`, and a stub agent that outlives a forwarded `SIGQUIT` proves the report.
- `npm run eval:preflight` staged no workspace for NFR, CI and test-design legs, so every live agent of those suites ran in an empty directory and reported its skill and project missing; `test/eval-contract-strength.js` now stages each leg's fixture set through the suite's own harness, as it already did for trace, and `npm run test:probe-corpus` asserts the staging for all four suites.
  The leg cache is keyed by how a suite's legs are staged, so a staging change can no longer answer a leg from a run in the old workspace, and `--force` runs each request live once, where it re-ran every leg that shares a request and overwrote the evidence an earlier leg was scored on.
- The NFR corpus's UNKNOWN manifestation witness read the bare word `UNKNOWN`, which a clean report may mention in prose, so it could fire on a clean leg; it now reads `**Threshold:** UNKNOWN`, the field the report template spells.
- Trace's step 4 read the gate target "from Step 1", but step 1 never resolved it; step 1 now resolves the gate type, target id and label, and step 4 reads them from the matrix frontmatter.
  Trace's step 5 both rewrote the whole matrix and said "do NOT overwrite it"; it now replaces the report body once, keeps the frontmatter, and only updates the frontmatter afterwards.
  A trace run that evaluates no gate removes an earlier `gate-decision-{run_key}.json` for the same scope, so a new summary is never paired with a stale decision.
- ATDD's resume step read `{outputFile}` without resolving `story_key` first; it now selects the checklist by story like test-design's resume.
- test-design's mode detection used `{implementation_artifacts}`, a BMM variable TEA never loads; it now names its source, `_bmad/bmm/config.yaml`, and falls back to `{output_folder}/implementation-artifacts`.
- Nine of the trace eval's skill-rule citations in `test/fixtures/trace-eval/ground-truth.json` (every step-05 gate rule, `gateEligibility`, `liveEvidenceCap`, and `liveBlockerSeverity`) pointed at the wrong lines, and the drift check missed them because each stayed inside its section; all eleven citations are re-pinned and `node test/eval-trace.js --validate-only` reports no drift.
  Six of the nfr eval's citations in `test/fixtures/nfr-eval/ground-truth.json` and three of the ci eval's in `test/fixtures/ci-eval/ground-truth.json` had drifted the same way; they are re-pinned, and both harnesses' `--validate-only` report no drift.
- `docs/how-to/workflows/run-trace.md` and `docs/how-to/brownfield/use-tea-for-enterprise.md` described a `gate-decision-{gate_type}-{story_id}.md` file that trace never wrote.
- `ci` and `framework` now apply the same re-run rule as the per-scope workflows: a completed checkpoint is replaced, an interrupted one asks whether to resume or start over, a headless run starts over, and two runs are never merged into one checkpoint.
  test-design gains headless branches: an interrupted checkpoint for the same scope starts over, and an epic-level run whose epic stays ambiguous halts with a message naming the candidate epics, since an epic plan has no system-level fallback.
  Trace now loads the NFR audit its checklist already expected, taking the first of `nfr/nfr-assessment-{run_key}.md`, the parent epic's `nfr/nfr-assessment-epic-{epic_num}.md` for a story, `nfr/nfr-assessment-system.md`, and the legacy root `nfr-assessment.md`.
  Automate finds an ATDD checklist when only the story id is known.
- `docs/how-to/workflows/run-trace.md` and `docs/how-to/brownfield/use-tea-for-enterprise.md` said trace reads test-review reports, and `run-trace.md`'s example gate decision showed a test quality score that trace never computes; both pages now list trace's real inputs, the test-design documents and the NFR audit.
- `npm run test:probe-targets` failed intermittently (and with it `test:doc-invocations`, which runs it again) whenever another process on the host held port 4310, for example a second worktree's `npm test`.
  The ATDD fixture's `playwright.config.ts` pointed its `webServer` at the default port 4310 while `tea-atdd-red-check` served the fixture on an OS-assigned port, so Playwright started a second server on 4310 for every spec file and failed to load it when the port was taken.
  The config now points `webServer` at the server `LOCKER_BASE_URL` names, so Playwright reuses it and starts nothing.
  The ATDD harness also crashed with "does not match its schema (harness bug)" when no spec file loaded, because it skipped an unmeasurable red-for-intended-reason rate; it now reports the rate as an unmeasurable quality failure, as the other harnesses do.
  `test:probe-targets` runs the correct-run case with port 4310 held and adds a load-error case.
- The runtime's relative-path pattern (a mutation's `targetArtifact` and a workspace `provision` entry) missed a `..` segment after a newline, because its lookahead used `.`; it now spans every character and refuses C0, DEL and C1 controls, the line and paragraph separators, bidirectional formatting characters and empty segments, and a `targetArtifact` may not end in a slash.
- `tea-evaluate check` and `digest` printed a finding's file name and message raw, so a corpus file name holding a newline printed a forged finding line; a control, line-separator or bidirectional formatting character now prints as an escape, and a file name holding one is quoted.
- The record validator (`createArtifactValidator`) registered no format, so Ajv warned and accepted any string as a `date-time`; it now registers one calendar-checked RFC 3339 `date-time` format from `cli/lib/evaluate/formats.js`, which `tea-evaluate check` shares.
- `redactSecrets` and `redactArgs` kept the userinfo of an http or https URL (`--endpoint=https://user:pass@host`); it is now redacted, keeping scheme and host.
- `digestFiles` digested a missing file and a present file whose bytes are exactly `<missing>` identically; the present file now contributes its path with a NUL appended, which no real path holds, so every other digest, a missing file's included, is unchanged.
- `npm run test:boundary` claimed to hold "no file under `cli/` imports from `test/`" and had no pattern for an import written without a file extension; its new `test-tree-reach` pattern refuses a relative climb or a self-referencing package path into `test/`, and `npm run test:layering-boundary-lineage` proves it on a seeded tree.
- `test/lib/probe-targets.js` built two default artifact paths with `path.join`, which yields backslashes on Windows that the registry schema refuses; they are POSIX literals now.
- `docs/explanation/how-tea-is-tested.md` described Evaluate as proposed future work with an undefined scope after it was registered as TEA's tenth workflow; the section now says what exists and links the new `tea-evaluate` reference. `README.md`'s `npm test` chain count reads seventy-nine for the two new checks, and the roadmap's list of TEA bins names `tea-evaluate` as the eleventh.
- Test and evaluation scripts whose color strings lacked the escape byte now print real ANSI colors in place of literal `[32m` text.
- `package.json`'s `files` array now carries negated `!src/workflows/testarch/*/.memlog.md` and `!src/workflows/testarch/*/.analysis/**` entries. Verified live: `npm pack` includes everything under a directory named in `files` regardless of `.gitignore` or `.npmignore`, so a stray `bmad-workflow-builder` session file left under a skill directory would have shipped in the published package; only a negated pattern inside `files` itself excludes it.

## [1.27.2] - 2026-09-18

### Added

- Added public explanation `docs/explanation/how-tea-is-tested.md` detailing deterministic checks, behavioral evaluations, clean and seeded fixture controls, gameability probes, repeated live runs, recorded results, exit classes, the TEA vs `eval-quality` boundary, and the planned Evaluate skill. Reconciled existing documentation to reflect full behavioral coverage across all ten skills.
- Live evaluation JSON now records a bounded diagnostic for every attempted case repetition, including the exact stability signature, threshold inputs, completion state, failure class, five-category root-cause triage, reason, and sanitized output or persisted artifact evidence. Schema 1.5.0 binds complete requested grids and aggregate failures to their diagnostic evidence while retaining frozen read-only validation paths for stored 1.4.0 and 1.3.0 results.

### Changed

- Deterministic live evaluation harnesses reject unsupported repetition counts during argument validation, before starting install, browser, or scoring work.
- The complete Story 1.8 live-baseline attempt is recorded with its environment result, protected 1.27.1 history remains byte-identical, and no quality score is claimed. Claude transport failures prevented measurable output, while the documented Codex substitution was unavailable under the local usage quota.

### Fixed

- Trace evaluation now repeats the resolved run metadata contract in its execution prompt and validates the required paths, empty URL fields, inventory counts, UI heuristic states, and explicit unknown handling during preflight. This keeps trace provenance complete and stable across repeated runs.
- Test-design guidance now grounds risks in supplied epic evidence, keeps risk registers within their declared scope, and requires exact risk IDs with suitable admitted test levels in coverage plans.
- NFR evidence audits now build one supplied-evidence ledger, bind every observation to a stable declared criterion ID, and publish every bound observation under exactly one criterion subsection in source order. Unsupported criteria receive one fixed structured gap, which prevents variable citation coverage or free-form gap labels across equivalent runs. Workers use canonical supplied-project-relative paths, scope findings to declared requirements, keep threshold sources separate from implementation evidence, and assign status strictly against each declared threshold. The replay scorer excludes Threshold Source lines from evidence and canonicalizes, de-duplicates, and sorts citations before grounding and stability signatures.
- Fragment selection now follows one closed selection contract across all eight evaluated TEA workflows. Each workflow starts from an empty set, evaluates complete stack, runner, package, and relevance gates, and loads only fragments named by matching rules. Deterministic checks reject every confirmed instruction ambiguity: tier-wide loading, flag-only Playwright Utils and Pact MCP gates, an unnamed Playwright Utils core set, nearby fragment references, and implicit contract relevance. They also hold every required oracle fragment to the workflow context that supplies it.
  The recorded Claude baseline remains unchanged with required recall 1, forbidden rate 0, and two unstable cases. Its investigation found a Python backend selecting `playwright-utils-mandate.md` against the existing oracle. A pre-change Codex substitution exposed the model-dependent form of the same ambiguity: all three framework cases selected different supersets across two repetitions, and the Python case selected `pactjs-utils-mandate.md` in both runs plus `pact-mcp.md` in one. The comparable Claude rerun was unavailable because that runner had reached its confirmed weekly usage limit; no further Claude calls were attempted. Bounded Codex reruns of the repaired framework, CI, and test-design contexts completed all 20 repetitions with required recall 1, forbidden rate 0, and zero unstable cases.
  Workflow-filtered fragment-selection runs now qualify diagnostic case identifiers with their workflow name. This closes the schema failure found while writing the pre-change Codex evidence and keeps focused JSON results tied to the manifest's declared case grid.
  Case-aware oracle validation rejects a required conditional fragment when the case config, runner, package, mobile stack, or contract-relevance facts do not open its positive load rule. Negative-only references cannot establish required ground truth.
- `bmad-tea` now asks one focused question when the supplied facts support several workflows. The routing guidance covers review versus trace, NFR evidence audit versus future test design, thin coverage across planning, automation, and measurement, and partially implemented scope across ATDD and automation. Direct requests to judge existing tests and recommend fixes route to Review Tests. Source-bound corpus validation, mutation checks, stored replays, and provenance-complete live evidence hold every ambiguity boundary and preserve clear and unservable routing behavior.
- The frozen schema 1.4.0 reader accepts later 1.4.0 diagnostics that emitted triage before the version advanced, while enforcing agreement with each diagnostic's failure class and root cause.
- ATDD red-phase generation now preserves supplied criterion ids, assigns stable collision-free ids to unnamed criteria, validates exactly one declared id per executable leaf, emits one transition-bearing red-phase leaf per criterion, defers secondary branches to green-phase automation, isolates each API criterion with a direct first assertion, keeps unimplemented setup responses opaque until that assertion, and gives E2E journeys a criterion-owned first failure boundary.

## [1.27.1] - 2026-09-17

### Added

- `test/lib/probe-targets.js`'s `probeCommandWithRetry` (TEA Story 5.2, stage two), a bounded retry over `probeCommand` for the two failure classes an ordinary network or CLI hiccup produces, `environment-timeout` and `environment-transport`. The retry set excludes `environment-configuration`, because the identical request has already been refused, and excludes `environment-parser` and `unexpected-error`, because another attempt cannot repair either condition. Found in the story's own first live run: one `tea-fragment-selection-runner` invocation hung past its declared budget and was killed, which lost the whole matrix even though the other hundreds of calls that run made all answered. At that volume, a per-call failure rate low enough to look rare still makes a fully clean run improbable, so Story 5.2's own AC1 ("every declared repetition completes") was gated on luck rather than reproducible.
  Scored calls complete on their first answered attempt. `probeCommand`'s `{ok: true}` means a real command answered; low scores and non-zero exits remain measurements. The bound permits three total attempts (`PROBE_RETRY_ATTEMPTS`), including the first. Every later attempt prints the interface, probe id, attempt number, and failure on `stderr`, so the transcript preserves the instability.
  All nine `test/eval-*.js` harnesses that call `probeCommand` for a live invocation, plus `test/lib/transcript-harness.js` (covering the `transcript` and `teach-me-testing` suites), now call `probeCommandWithRetry` instead; the two suites that never went through `probeCommand` at all, `automate` and `framework`, spawn Playwright and `npm install` directly and are unaffected. `test/test-probe-targets.js`'s `checkProbeRetry` proves every branch with fake ports: a retryable failure that recovers on the bound's last attempt reports the success and prints one retry line per failed attempt; a retryable failure that persists uses exactly the declared attempt bound and reports the final failure; a non-retryable class and a first-attempt success each run once and print nothing.
- The first complete live `eval:all` result is recorded for TEA Story 5.2 at repository commit `5c84a77f19576b075513dbbea9a74821cfe19f5d`, using `eval-quality` 3.4.0 and Claude Code 2.1.273 with the resolved `sonnet` model.
  All 109 declared repetitions across twelve suites completed in 9,830,418 ms with zero unaccounted skills and no environment failure.
  The run exited `1` with `failureClass: "quality"`: `automate`, `ci`, `test-review`, `transcript`, `framework`, and `teach-me-testing` met every threshold; `atdd`, `bmad-tea-routing`, `fragment-selection`, `nfr`, `test-design`, and `trace` recorded the measured quality findings in their suite results.
  `test/results/eval-all/latest.json` and `test/results/eval-all/history/2026-09-17T12-23-09-218Z.json` preserve the complete unedited run, including runner, model, versions, corpus and prompt digests, measurements, durations, and failure classes.

### Fixed

- Live probe retries for CI, NFR, test-design, and trace now create a fresh staged workspace and command port for every attempt, so an attempt that times out or loses transport cannot leave artifacts or progress checkpoints for a later successful attempt to score. Stored `eval:all` records also normalize runner executables to repository-relative paths or basenames, keeping the committed evidence portable without changing its measurements.
