- `tea-test-review` spent most of a small review reading files that were not in the pull request. `step-02-discover-tests.md` §2b's convention sample was capped at 40 files, and the agent runs with no shell, so each sampled file cost its own `Read` turn on every run whatever the size of the diff. The agent now reads 8. Measured on a 50-file corpus with a one-file review set: 40 files and ~233 KB before, 8 files and ~47 KB after, five times fewer reads and a fifth of the bytes. Eight clears §2b's `sampled < 4` "corpus too small to infer a house rule" floor with margin and leaves the 0.5 established/emerging ratio a denominator that means something. The sampled count is published in the report and cross-checked by `parse-report.js`, so every report states the corpus it was judged against.
- The mechanical detector scan keeps the corpus it always had. It is a separate budget from the read set: `cli/lib/convention-baseline.js` opens those files itself and pays no agent turn, so it still scans the closest 40 while the agent reads 8 of them. `mechanicalSignal: false` is what forbids a report from citing any adoption for a key, and a zero observed over 8 files is much weaker evidence of absence than a zero observed over 40. Measured on this repository before the two budgets were split, `networkFirst` went from 1 of 40 to 0 of 8, which turns `emerging` into `absent` and removes the deduction entirely.
- The 8 are spread evenly across the closest 40. Every file in the reviewed file's own directory ranks at distance 0, so among those the ranking is the alphabetical tie-break and nothing else. At a cap of 40 against a 45-file corpus that never showed; at 8 the tie-break became the selector. Measured on this repository, a review anchored in `test/` sampled `eval-all.js` through `test-contracts.js` and nothing named `test-e*` or later was reachable by any review anchored there, on any run. Taking every `floor(i × 40 / 8)`-th file keeps the count, the determinism and the neighborhood, and the sample now spans `test/`, `test/lib/`, `test/schema/` and `test/fixtures/`.
- The convention corpus counts a file only when its name says it is a test, and skips `fixtures`, `__fixtures__`, `__mocks__` and `testdata` trees. `isTestFile` is deliberately wider, because the review set has to catch a changed helper under a test directory, and a directory-only match was never right for a baseline: on this repository it admitted `stub-agent.js` and a React component under `trace-eval/clean/src/tokens/`, and it admitted the seeded-defect specs under `test/fixtures/test-review-eval/seeded/` that the evals score against, so the house convention was partly established from deliberate anti-patterns. This repository's own corpus is now empty and its self-review reports the baseline as unavailable, which is the truthful answer for a repository whose only spec files are eval fixtures.
- The four quality workers in `step-03-quality-evaluation.md` can now actually run in parallel. Three things had to change together. `cli/lib/build-prompt.js` pinned `tea_execution_mode=sequential`; the `claude` adapter's tool list carried no delegation tool, so step-03's capability probe found no launcher; and `tea_capability_probe` was never stated at all, which resolves `auto` to `sequential` on every run whatever the mode says. The prompt now states both orchestration keys, and `cli/lib/agent-adapters.js` grants `Task` from the `scoped-artifact-writes` tier upward in both `--tools` and `--allowedTools`. A launched subagent inherits that list exactly, verified live against claude 2.1.266: the child reported `Agent, Edit, Glob, Grep, Read, Write` and no shell, so "the shell only under command-execution" holds at the workers too. `read-only` runners get no delegation, since the workers' declared outputs cannot exist at that tier, and `codex exec` exposes no subagent launcher, so `auto` resolves to `sequential` there and that adapter's run is unchanged.
- `tea_execution_mode` and `tea_capability_probe` resolve through the same chain the fragment keys do: `--execution-mode` / `--capability-probe` / `--no-capability-probe`, then `_bmad/tea/config.yaml`, then `src/module.yaml`. Both were previously stated in the prompt as literals, and the prompt declares that stated values beat `config.yaml`, so `tea_execution_mode: sequential` in the file that `docs/reference/troubleshooting.md` tells users to edit was being ignored.
- The report carries a `**Execution Mode**:` line and the verdict JSON carries `executionMode`, required on any run that measured a convention baseline. step-03 resolves its mode from a runtime capability probe and printed the result only to the agent's stdout, which the CLI reads only when the report is missing, so a run that asked for parallel workers and silently got `sequential` was indistinguishable afterwards from one that got what it asked for. `step-03f-aggregate-scores.md` also stopped hardcoding `subagent_execution: 'PARALLEL (4 quality dimensions)'` and `performance_gain: '~60% faster than sequential'` regardless of what ran.
- The CLI mints a `tea_run_id` and the workflow uses it as the timestamp in its four worker output paths. step-03 asked the agent to generate one with `new Date().toISOString()`, which an agent with no clock and no shell cannot execute, so it emitted a plausible string; nothing cleans `/tmp/tea-test-review-*` and step-03 checks only that the four files exist, so two runs landing on the same invented value would aggregate each other's scores.
- The default agent wall-clock timeout scales with the review set: 20 minutes plus 2 minutes per reviewed file, capped at the 30 minutes it used to be flat at, with `--timeout-ms` still overriding it. Nothing bounds how many turns the agent takes and no supported vendor CLI offers a turn cap (`claude` 2.1.266 has `--max-budget-usd` and no `--max-turns`; `codex exec` has neither), so the wall clock is the only vendor-agnostic bound. A one-file review that stopped making progress used to be indistinguishable from a slow one for half an hour.

### Fixed

- Three statements of the module defaults that disagreed with `src/module.yaml` and with `cli/lib/resolve-tea-config.js`'s `MODULE_DEFAULTS`, which a test already holds equal to each other. `--use-pactjs-utils`'s help said the default is `false` and `--pact-mcp`'s said `none`; both are `true` and `"mcp"`. `docs/reference/tea-test-review-cli.md` repeated the same two wrong values and then advised the opposite of what the CLI does, telling a contract-testing repo it gets `tea_use_pactjs_utils: false` by default and should pass `--use-pactjs-utils` to change it. A repo on raw `@pact-foundation/pact` needs `--no-use-pactjs-utils`.

## [1.25.2] - 2026-09-09

### Fixed

- `trace`'s clean control scored FAIL at exit 2 because five of its twenty-six oracles abstained. `O-009`, `O-010`, `O-011`, `O-013` and `O-014` are `for-any` quantifiers over the seeded set's summary, and `P-004`'s record carried one observation that both plan steps selected, so those five quantified over the clean summary's empty or absent collections. Two changes close it and they work only together: each plan step binds `stdin.prompt` as a literal of that set's own prompt, and the clean control's record carries both fixture sets' runs. Measured on the stored replay, `P-004` moves from FAIL at exit 2 with five abstentions to CONCERNS at exit 0 with all twenty-six oracles resolving `passed-clean-control`; four unsatisfied AD-20 coverage rules hold it at CONCERNS and are separate work. Either half alone is worse than neither, and both were run before this was accepted: two observations under the old matcher bindings leave every observation satisfying both steps, so `exactly-one` reports selector ambiguity on all twenty-six oracles at exit 3; literals over one observation select nothing, so all twenty-six resolve `unreached` and the run reports CONCERNS at exit 0 having examined no evidence, which is a silent green worse than the FAIL it replaces. A literal is compared with `deepEquals`, so the contract's literal and the record's prompt are both `buildPrompt` from `test/eval-trace.js`, and `traceEvidence` throws when the contract on disk binds any other bytes. The record used to carry the placeholder `The prompt the trace harness assembles for <case>.`, which is a prompt no run has ever sent. The control still catches a false positive: a clean summary damaged to derive FAIL with an invented blocker, reported as a defect finding, resolves `O-016` to `false-positive` and the probe to FAIL at exit 2. `test/contracts/trace.contract.json` grows 3.6 kilobytes, the size of the two prompts; the eight fragment-selection contracts keep the matcher binding, where the same fix costs 42 to 143 kilobytes each and roughly doubles every file.
- A stale reason in `test/contracts/README.md`, surfaced by the contradiction the entry above created. The paragraph on `trace.contract.json`'s witness said its two plan steps share one prompt on purpose because `test/eval-trace.js` names no fact about either fixture set in it, and it said the witness legs are runnable only against a staged workspace of the seeded set. Both stopped being true one release earlier: each set declares its own `projectRoot`, the whole prompt is written against it, and both witness legs stage the clean set. The `allow_gate` reasoning, the differential-and-invariance argument, and the pointer to `testData.setup` all survive and are kept. `testData.setup` in the contract itself was already correct and needed no change.
- A stale illustration in `docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md`. The passage on AD-4 said `trace`'s five `for-any` oracles over the seeded export "still abstain on a clean run", which stopped being true once each plan step selected its own set's run. AD-4's rule is unchanged and the sentence now dates the abstention it describes.

## [1.25.1] - 2026-09-09

### Changed

- `eval-quality` moves from 1.3.0 to 1.4.0, which closes three findings this repository raised against it, and the probe corpora record what changed. Measured on the stored replay across the two versions: `test-review`'s five plants that had failed pre-flight on `seeded-faults-scoped` with a null verdict and exit 3 now pass pre-flight and score, taking the suite's defect class from four exercised and four caught to nine and nine at a rate of 1; `trace`'s O-023 and O-024 move from `abstained` to `passed-clean-control`, because a bare `count-tolerance` with `expected: 0` now counts a collection observed to be present and empty; and `trace`'s three defect probes report `condition-artifact-channel-contract-local`, the reason AD-9's gate refused them, where before a rejected probe surfaced only as `infrastructure-error` and exit 3. `expected-strength.json` records a `qualification` field per probe, so a rejection that changes its reason shows up as a diff.
- Each trace fixture set is staged under its own project root, declared as `projectRoot` in `test/fixtures/trace-eval/ground-truth.json` and named for the epic the set traces: `tenant-data-export` and `api-token-lifecycle`. Both sets used to stage at one `project/` root and `buildPrompt` read nothing off the set it was given, so the two sets sent byte-identical prompts and the staged workspace, which is the run's real input, was nameable from no request. The whole prompt is written against the set's root now, so a leg can ask for the set it means: `stagedWorkspaceFor` stages the set the prompt names, the stored-evidence port answers with that set's stored run, and the stub agent resolves `{project-root}` off the prompt the way a real agent does. `validateCorpus` fails a corpus whose two sets share a root or whose root names the set's role, because a run that read `clean` in the directory it works in would have been handed the answer.

### Fixed

- Four passages describing limitations that no longer exist. `test/probes/README.md` and `docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md` each said a rejected probe carries no reason across the boundary; the adapter document also said "this collection is empty" has no spelling, and argued that no leg TEA could add repairs `seeded-faults-scoped` firing on a leg carrying the fault leg's own request. All four are rewritten against what 1.4.0 does, with the measured before and after. One finding stays open and belongs to TEA rather than to `eval-quality`: `trace`'s three defect probes address a file the command wrote, which the qualification gate refuses by design. The other is closed below.
- `trace`'s three defect probes failed pre-flight on `seeded-faults-scoped`, reported as `D-001: the manifestation witness fires on clean leg "witness-gate-withheld"`. The witness was right and the leg was not clean. AD-10 reads every other leg of an operation as a clean leg, `trace-fixture-set` declares `stateChangeMarker: true` so no control leg is planned for it, and the only other legs it had were its two sensitivity witness legs, both staging the seeded set. The manifestation witness asserts a coverage number the seeded workspace produces and `allow_gate` does not move, so it fired there truthfully. Both witness legs stage the clean set now, where P0 coverage is 100 and the relation asserting 50 resolves false, so the check is satisfied on evidence rather than on an authored relation. The `allow_gate` differential is unchanged and holds on either set. Measured on the stored replay, the three probes move from `preflight: failed: seeded-faults-scoped` to `preflight: passed` and lose the `pre-flight verdict did not pass` line from their basis; they are still refused by the qualification gate, so their verdict, exit code and strength are unchanged. All thirty-one probes in the corpus pre-flight now.
- Every signature channel `tea-trace-runner` could carry, measured through `runScore` rather than asserted: an `artifact` pointer is refused as `condition-artifact-channel-contract-local`, a `stdout` pointer as `condition-pointer-unwritable`, and an `exit-code` condition is admitted and discriminates nothing, since `cli/lib/runner-exit-codes.js` gives 0 to every run whose agent completed. The three probes keep the signature that states the truth about the plant, and `test/probes/README.md` and `docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md` name the reason code rather than describing the refusal in prose.
- Stale numbers and claims throughout `docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md`, checked against `test/probes/expected-strength.json` and against the contracts rather than reread. Its corpus table was pinned at `eval-quality` 1.3.0 and said `test-review` caught four of four with five plants firing on a witness leg; the baseline says nine of nine. It said eight probes cannot be pre-flighted; none is left. It said seven of `trace`'s twenty-six oracles abstain on the clean control; five do, measured on `P-004`, and all five are `for-any` quantifiers over the seeded export named in the artifact's own `verdictBasis`. It said no request shape names a trace fixture set, which the project root now does. The dated live pre-flight table is kept as the measurement it was, with what has moved under it since recorded beside it, including that `trace` now spawns three legs where it spawned two because its manifestation witnesses no longer share a request with a witness leg.

## [1.25.0] - 2026-09-09

### Added

- `npm run test:contract-oracles`, which evaluates every oracle in every Behavioral Evaluation Contract with `eval-quality`'s own evaluator and fails when an oracle faults or disagrees with the harness scorer on the same evidence. Every stored `test-review` verdict under `test/replay/` is one observation of the contract's `review-corpus` step, and every fragment-selection case is evaluated over three constructed selections plus the stored captures, 289 checks in all. Nothing had read an oracle before: `test:contracts` compiles a contract and never runs an operator, and `test:contract-sources` checks the JSON matches its generator. Its first run found that eleven of the thirteen oracles in `test-review.contract.json` could not be evaluated at all; see Fixed.
- `--preflight-only` on `eval:fragment-selection` and `eval:trace`, which runs the static data checks and then probes the runner: the executable is on `PATH`, answers `--version`, and a built-in vendor has a credential. It exits before any model call. `eval:test-review` already had the flag, and the suite manifest now declares it as `preflightArgs` for all three suites. `--validate-only` is unchanged and stays the credential-free mode `npm test` runs.
- Runner capabilities are applied, and checked. `cli/lib/run-agent.js` takes a `capabilities` list in the suite manifest's own words, and each built-in adapter turns the strongest tier into vendor argv: under `read-only` claude runs with `--tools Read,Glob,Grep` and codex with `--sandbox read-only`; the default tier is the one the review CLI has always run at, so its argv is unchanged byte for byte; only `command-execution` adds `Bash`. Each eval harness exports the `RUNNER_CAPABILITIES` it hands to its runner, and `npm run test:eval-schemas` fails when the manifest declares a different set, the same way it fails on a threshold the harness does not apply.
- A thirteenth replay case, `test/replay/test-review/out-of-scope-finding`, whose stored verdict carries one finding against `src/orders.service.ts`, the implementation the seeded spec imports and nobody asked the reviewer to look at. It is the case that exercises the scope control `ground-truth.json` had recorded as "not exercised" since the corpus was written.
- `tea-test-review --gate-on introduced|all`. Git-diff pull-request reviews default to `introduced`: findings in added files or pure-addition hunks are `introduced`, findings in replacement hunks are `modified`, and findings outside changed lines are `pre_existing`. Every JSON finding carries that provenance, its changed-line evidence, and `verdict_impact`; pre-existing findings appear in a report advisory section and are removed from the gating recommendation, severity counts, score floor, and Critical cap. `--gate-on all` preserves full baseline-audit behavior, and explicit `--files` reviews default to it because they deliberately skip git evidence.
- The first Behavioral Evaluation Contracts, under `test/contracts/`: one for the `test-review` behavioral eval and eight for fragment selection, 24 cases across eight workflows. A contract states what a skill has to do in a vocabulary that lives outside this repository, so TEA owns the fixtures, the seeded defects and the runners, and `eval-quality` owns the format and the compiler. `npm run test:contracts` checks each one against the status `test/contracts/expected-status.json` records for it, and a status that moves in either direction fails until the baseline is updated. Every contract was `blocked` when they were written, because the contract language could only describe a system under test that speaks HTTP and a TEA skill runs behind a command. `test/contracts/README.md` records that finding with its evidence, which is the entry criterion `eval-quality`'s own release plan asked for. Later entries in this section carry the versions that closed it.
- Criteria registry row `H10`, Shape-only assertion, HIGH and Absolute. It fires when every assertion about a value the system produced constrains its type, its class, or its presence, and none constrains the value. `test-quality.md` teaches it, and the enforcement hook defers it because deciding it needs every assertion in a test read together.
- `npm run test:ci-coverage`, which fails when a check in the `npm test` chain runs nowhere in CI. The GitHub Actions validate job runs each check as its own step rather than running the chain, so a failure names itself in the job list, and that makes the workflow a transcription of the chain. It had already drifted: four checks added in one change reached `npm test` and never reached the workflow, so contract drift, a broken replay record and a corrupted trace corpus would each have passed CI while failing on a laptop.
- Deterministic replay tests at `test/replay/` and `npm run test:eval-replay`, thirteen stored outputs scored with no model call and no network. Each case records the result scoring it must reproduce, derived by hand from the ground truth rather than generated from the code under test. A scorer or parser change either reproduces every stored result or declares itself: the harness carries a scorer version, a mismatch names the case and the field that moved, and `--accept` refuses to re-record until that version is bumped in a one-line edit somebody has to review. Each `test-review` case also pins a digest over the scoring inputs, so a moved plant fails with its own message rather than reading as a scorer regression. Two of the thirteen stored outputs are real captures from live agent runs; the other eleven are constructed, because the only real outputs this repository has ever banked are both unscoreable.
- A behavioral evaluation corpus for `trace` at `test/fixtures/trace-eval/`, with a seeded set of ten acceptance criteria whose expected gate is FAIL and a clean set of five whose expected gate is PASS. Its ground truth records each criterion's true coverage, the evidence span that establishes it, the coverage arithmetic, and the gate rule that produces the expected decision. The discriminating case is a test named for a criterion whose assertions establish none of it, which a run matching on names scores as covered.
- Replay coverage for the `trace` scorers: fourteen stored cases under `test/replay/trace/`, each a constructed `e2e-trace-summary.json` and `traceability-matrix.md` for one fixture set, read through `readSummary` and `readMatrix` and scored by `scoreRun` and the eight scorers it calls, with no model call and no network. Roughly seven hundred lines of scoring logic in `test/eval-trace.js` had no regression test while every other scorer under `test/replay/` had one. The cases cover a correct run of each set, both discriminating false positives with the gate flipping from FAIL to PASS on the seeded set and from PASS to FAIL on the clean one, a percentage that is not the rounding of its own counts, a missing oracle source, an omitted `rejected_evidence` array, a waiver turned down for two reasons out of six, a matrix full of lines the parser must ignore, an invented and a duplicated criterion section, an empty `waivers` block on the set that has no register, and two artifacts the harness must refuse to score at all. Every expected result was derived by hand from the ground truth. Each case digests its own fixture set's scoring inputs, so a moved evidence span fails with its own message, and the cases hold `signatureOf` to its contract: two cases of one set sign identically exactly when their results are identical, and a counted fixture mutation always moves the signature. `SCORER_VERSION` is 4 and the thirteen earlier cases reproduce unchanged. The corpus is twenty-seven cases, twenty-three of which produce a number, and the check runs through the existing `npm run test:eval-replay` step in `npm test` and in CI.
- The `trace` eval harness scores six fields `e2e-trace-summary.json` already carried and no check read: `oracle.confidence`, `oracle.sources`, `decision_mode`, `links.*`, `tests.skipped_cases`/`fixme_cases`/`pending_cases`, and `heuristics.ui_journey_status`/`ui_state_status`. Each has exactly one correct value over this corpus: `oracle.confidence` and `oracle.sources` are the same oracle the ground truth already records, `decision_mode` and `links.trace_report_path` are the workflow's own config defaults, the three test-hygiene counts are zero because neither fixture set plants a skipped, fixme, or pending test, and both UI heuristics read `not_applicable` because neither fixture's oracle is synthetic. `target`, `evaluator`, `source_sha`, and `recommendations` text stay unscored: each depends on a runtime extraction, an operator identity, a live git commit, or free text this corpus has no fixture-carried fact to check against, and a wrong guess here would fail a correct run.

- A versioned eval suite manifest at `test/evals/suite-manifest.json`, with its Zod schema at `test/schema/suite-manifest.js`. Each suite declares its skills, eval type, fixtures, ground truth, contract, thresholds, repetition count, CI tier, and runner capabilities. Every skill with no behavioral suite carries a deferred entry naming its owner, the missing evidence, and the condition that retires it. `npm run eval:all` discovers its suites from that file and exits `2` when a TEA skill appears in neither list, so a suite list can no longer imply coverage that does not exist.
- `--json <path>` on `eval:all`, `eval:fragment-selection`, and `eval:test-review`. The record carries the repository commit, the suite and case IDs, the runner executable and version, the resolved model and parameters, the fixture and prompt digests, the expected and completed repetitions, the measurements, the duration, and the final failure class. Every record is validated against `test/schema/eval-result.schema.json` before it is written.
- `npm run test:eval-schemas`, a deterministic check that validates the manifest, confirms each declared threshold is the one its harness actually applies, verifies the declared case counts against the fixtures, and fails when a TEA skill is unaccounted for.
- The `tea-test-review` JSON verdict carries a `findings` array: one entry per finding block the report documents, with the severity read from its `criteria-registry.md` row, the row id, the file, the line, the section, and the title. `violations` is four severity counts, so anything needing to know WHICH defects a review reported had to re-parse the markdown report to find out. Per severity the array agrees with `violations` — exactly for Critical and High, never exceeding it for Medium and Low — and a disagreement is a parse failure (exit 3). A finding whose `**Location**:` line is missing or unreadable still counts and still reaches the verdict, with a null file and line rather than a guessed one.
- Stable finding fields in the `tea-test-review` JSON verdict: `criterion_id`, `severity`, `path`, `line`, `provenance`, `deduction`, and `verdict_impact`. Existing `row` and `file` fields remain as compatibility aliases. Reports without changed-line evidence use `provenance: "unknown"` rather than a guessed classification.
- Review provenance in verdict and skip JSON and in PR comments: TeA CLI and skill/rubric versions, model identifier, base/head commits, trigger comment, workflow run, and gate mode. Unavailable values are `null`, with a source or fallback explanation in the JSON.
- `tea-trace-runner` (`cli/trace-runner.js`), the command the trace behavioral suite had no equivalent of. `test/eval-trace.js` was the last harness spawning its own agent, and the only one with no command to point at: `tea-test-review` and `tea-fragment-selection-runner` both exist and a trace runner did not. Its whole surface is a prompt on standard input and an agent run in the working directory, leaving the workflow's two artifacts on disk for the caller's artifact map. It builds no prompt and knows no vendor, declares `scoped-artifact-writes` because a trace run writes its deliverables and a selection does not, and spells its exit codes as TEA's failure classes. Registered in `test/lib/probe-targets.js` with a twenty-one-minute backstop over the harness's twenty-minute clock, so the inner clock classifies and the outer one only kills; declared as a bin in `package.json`.
- `test/contracts/trace.contract.json`, the tenth Behavioral Evaluation Contract and the first for `trace`, whose suite had declared `"contracts": []`. Every one of its 26 oracles states a value `test/fixtures/trace-eval/ground-truth.json` records: the gate and its ten criteria fields, the inventory, the priority rows, the risk counts, the per-level criteria counts, the live dispositions and each blocker's severity, the rejected span at the corpus's line tolerance, and each waiver's verdict, for both fixture sets. Its sensitivity witness is a differential over standard input on `allow_gate`, because the two plan steps share one prompt and a differential between them would attribute to the prompt a difference the staged workspace produced; step-05 writes `gate_basis` as `none` when the gate is withheld, which is the one prompt value the corpus establishes an effect for. `npm run test:contract-oracles` evaluates all 26 over the fourteen stored trace runs under both sets' oracles and compares each with the `scoreRun` check it restates, 296 evaluations, and requires every oracle to be seen failing on some stored run.
- `cli/lib/runner-exit-codes.js`, the one exit-code table both runner commands share. A caller holding a probe observation cannot tell which command produced it, so two commands spelling one failure class with two numbers would leave it guessing; `tea-fragment-selection-runner` imports the table it used to own, `tea-trace-runner` imports the same one, and `test:probe-targets` asserts the two exports are equal.
- `test/fixtures/trace-runner/stub-agent.js`, a vendor stand-in for the trace runner. It copies the replay corpus's own correct-run artifacts into the workspace's `test-artifacts/`, chosen by which epic the workspace carries, and answers `--version`, so the whole trace harness runs end to end with no credential: `npm run test:probe-targets` spawns `test/eval-trace.js` against it and reads the result record back, every threshold met on the correct run, a `quality` failure naming fixture mutations when the stub writes a test, and `environment-missing-artifact` when it writes nothing.
- `tea-fragment-selection-runner`, the command the eight fragment-selection contracts already named. Nothing by that name existed, which is worse than a declared gap: the contract compiles, pre-flight schedules a leg against it, and the gate stays green over a command nobody can run. Its whole surface is the one turn those contracts declare, a prompt on standard input and `{"fragments": [...]}` on standard output. It builds no prompt and knows no vendor: the prompt belongs to the eval corpus and the vendor call is `cli/lib/run-agent.js`. Its exit codes are TEA's own failure classes, so a caller reading an exit code off a probe observation lands on the class it would have derived from a thrown error.
- `test/lib/probe-targets.js`, TEA's execution-target registry and the `eval-quality` environment-probe port over it. It declares which real file each logical executable resolves to, builds the `CommandTargetPolicy` that authorizes it, creates the adapter, and maps every fault the port can throw onto a TEA failure class, so a killed run is classified and never scored. A logical name with no entry is denied before a process starts.
- `npm run test:probe-targets`, which drives both real TEA commands through the real adapter and the real policy against the checked-in fixtures, with a stub standing in for the vendor. It makes no model call and needs no credential. It asserts default-deny in four directions, the observation shape, artifact read-back as JSON and as text, an artifact nothing wrote reported as `absent`, a real budget kill classified as a timeout, and that every contract's declared interface, executable, and subcommand path is one the registry carries, in both directions.
- [The eval-quality Command Adapter in TEA](docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md), recording which invented machinery the adapter replaces, which stays and why, the two path couplings and three expressiveness limits found by running it, and the per-skill order the remaining commands land in.
- [Adopting eval-quality, One Skill at a Time](docs/explanation/eval-quality-adoption-guide.md), a working guide for an engineer on another BMAD module who has never used `eval-quality` and wants one skill measured. It states what has to exist before anything can be measured (an observable output and a command-line entry point, which was the single biggest cost here: two of TEA's three commands were written so contracts had something real to name), how to build ground truth for one skill, why a clean control is mandatory, what a threshold means and where it is declared twice, the rule that a failed model call is an environment failure and never a low score, what the contract compiler catches and what it cannot, how to run the suite deterministically and live, what a full matrix costs in model calls and wall clock, and which parts stay manual. It carries the mistakes with the successes: the three measured `trace` runs that disagreed with the corpus and were right, the prompt silently truncated at 8 KB, the three declarations nothing enforced, and the eleven of thirteen oracles that compiled clean and could never be evaluated. It also says what does not transfer, because TEA's contracts lean on artifacts TEA happens to write and a skill whose only output is conversation is a different problem.
- Probe corpora at `test/probes/`, and `tools/generate-probes.js` that writes them. A contract says what a skill has to do; a probe says what was wrong with the system when the contract was asked, and `runScore` reads the two together to answer the question `eval-quality` exists for. Thirty-one probes across ten corpora, every one generated from ground truth this repository already keeps: the nine planted registry rows as `defect` probes with real controlled-mutation qualification records, the three coverage gaps the seeded trace set withholds, a clean control for every contract, and a gameability probe for each fragment-selection contract and for `test-review`. Every probe names the oracle that catches it, and that is enforced rather than intended: the generator reads `behaviorId` out of the contract and refuses a probe whose behavior discharges more than one oracle. `npm run test:probe-sources` fails when a corpus on disk differs from what its sources generate.
- `npm run test:probe-corpus`, which runs the whole scoring half end to end with no model call: every probe validated against the schema `eval-quality` publishes for it, `runPreflight` planning and reducing each probe's legs through a port that answers from the stored outputs under `test/replay/`, `runScore` returning a verdict and a contract-strength vector, `seal` producing a brief, and every artifact the chain mints validated against the published JSON Schema again on the way out. `test/probes/expected-strength.json` records what all thirty-one probes score, and movement in either direction fails until somebody has read why. The first run reported three findings about the contracts rather than about the runs: `test-review` leaves three AD-20 coverage rules unsatisfied and each fragment-selection contract leaves one, `trace`'s clean control scores FAIL because seven of its oracles quantify over collections the clean set leaves empty and abstain, and a plan whose steps bind their inputs by matcher cannot tell those steps apart on a record carrying one observation.
- `npm run eval:preflight` and `npm run eval:contract-strength`, the live half. `runPreflight` drives every contract's witness legs through the real command-line adapter against the commands TEA ships, which is the first pre-flight this repository has ever run: twenty legs against `claude` on 2026-09-09, 46 minutes of model time, and every witness held for a reason the corpus establishes. Every observation is cached under a digest of the request that produced it, so a rate limit costs one leg and not the set, and the same key collapses the legs that are the same request: AD-10 mints two control-observe legs from the first witness leg's inputs, so eight planned legs cost two runs. Each spawned leg runs in its own staged workspace, never in the repository, because the first live trace pre-flight failed its own witness on a summary the previous leg had left in a shared one. `test/eval-artifacts/contract-strength/preflight-cost.json` records what each run spent.
- `npm run test:probe-conformance`, which runs `eval-quality`'s own published port conformance suite against the adapter TEA probes its commands through. Fifteen assertions, the package's rather than TEA's: an unmapped interface, an unmapped executable, an unauthorized subcommand path, a non-zero exit read as an observation, a shell-metacharacter argument proven to reach the process as one literal token, a declared artifact captured, both caps enforced, and the six shared port assertions. `test/fixtures/probe-conformance/fixture-command.js` is the real executable the command-specific assertions run against, because a synthetic process proves nothing about argv construction, a wall clock, or an output cap. No model call and no credential.
- `test/lib/eval-quality-inputs.js` and `test/lib/probe-scoring.js`, the one place the six inputs `runScore` takes are assembled and the one place the pre-flight, score and seal stages are wired. Every artifact TEA builds is checked against `eval-quality/schemas/*` with `ajv`, a new devDependency pinned at the version `eval-quality` itself uses, so the published interchange schema is read independently of the package's own parse.

### Changed

- `.nvmrc` is Node 24 and `engines.node` is `>=22.20.0`. Every workflow reads `.nvmrc`, so CI moves with it. The floor moved because it was false: `eval-quality` 1.2.0 requires `>=22.20.0`, so a fresh install anywhere in 22.0 through 22.19 got a dependency declaring it could not run there, which is what `npm install` reported on a 22.12.0 machine. The two supported versions are still the current LTS and the one before it.
- `csv-parse` moved from `^6.1.0` to `^7.0.2` and `markdownlint-cli2` from `^0.19.1` to `^0.23.2`, and `npm audit fix` took the rest of the semver-compatible advisories. The graph went from 28 known vulnerabilities to 6. The six that remain are the Astro documentation-site graph, where the fix is Astro 5 to 7, two major versions; they are recorded here rather than taken silently.
- `package-lock.json` records `tea-trace-runner` as a declared bin. The command shipped without the lockfile catching up.
- `tools/build-docs.js` excludes the eval-quality adoption guide from `llms-full.txt`, on the reasoning that already excludes the roadmap and the command-adapter document: they tell an agent consuming TEA to do testing work nothing. Measured both ways. The bundle is 571,631 characters without it and 615,953 with it, against a 600,000 character cap the file marks DO NOT CHANGE, so including it fails the build.
- `npm run eval:preflight` and `npm run eval:contract-strength` read their exit code against `test/probes/expected-strength.json`, the baseline the deterministic gate already compares to: 0 when every probe reached the outcome the corpus records, 1 when a verdict moved, 2 when a pre-flight outcome moved. The check used to read a verdict, and in `--preflight-only` there is no verdict to read, so a run where every leg failed reported success from the one flag whose whole purpose is to fail before a paid matrix starts. Exiting 2 on any failed pre-flight fixes that and replaces it: eight of the thirty-one probes cannot be pre-flighted for reasons no leg TEA can author repairs, so the script would be red on every run, and a script that is always red stops being read before the day it means something. Movement in either direction is the finding, which is the idiom `test/contracts/expected-status.json` already uses.
- The live pre-flight observation cache is keyed on the agent as well as the suite. `requestKey` runs before the agent is merged into the request's option channel, so two vendors shared one entry and a second run against a different agent would have been answered by the first while reporting itself cached. Credentials stay out of the key; the agent is the input that changes the answer.
- `tools/generate-probes.js` writes repository-relative paths with POSIX separators. It hashes the path string beside the file's bytes and stores it in the generated probe, so a Windows checkout would have regenerated every digest and reported a stale corpus on a tree nobody had touched. On POSIX the change is the identity and no committed byte moved.
- `test/fixtures/probe-conformance/fixture-command.js` sets `process.exitCode` and exits naturally. The adapter captures it through a pipe, so `process.exit` could abandon an undrained write, and the case that writes past the output cap is exactly the one a truncated stream would turn into a passing assertion for the wrong reason. `test/test-probe-conformance.js` removes its staged workspace in a `finally`, so a throw out of the suite no longer leaves one behind per failed invocation.
- `eval-quality` devDependency moved from `1.2.0` to `1.3.0`, which opened `Defect.manifestationWitness.inputs` and `FixtureReset.inputs` to the same union a sensitivity-witness leg already took. Before it, that field was the HTTP-only `WitnessInputs`, so a manifestation witness against a command was unrepresentable in both directions: command channels failed the Probe parse and transport channels threw `undeclared-mandatory-input` at plan time. A `null` witness fails the `seeded-fault-fired` check, which fails pre-flight, which invalidates the run, so no defect probe could be scored at all. All twelve declare a witness now. Four of the nine `test-review` plants pre-flight cleanly and the defect class catches all four; the other five and all three `trace` plants fire on a leg `planPreflight` calls clean, which is `seeded-faults-scoped` answering AD-10's question against the contract's own sensitivity witness legs.
- `trace.contract.json` declares one behavior per oracle, twenty-six of each, minted from the oracle identifiers. Its two probes passed the fallthrough before only because a clean control resolves `passed-clean-control` on every oracle; a defect probe would have been voted by `O-001` whatever criterion it withheld.
- The two `trace` oracles that assert an empty collection spell it `count-tolerance` with `expected: 0` rather than `deep-equality` against a literal `[]`. Neither resolves: the evaluator intercepts an empty array on every quantifier and every single-operand leaf and returns `insufficient-evidence` before the operator runs, so "this collection is empty" has no spelling and the clean control scores FAIL on two oracles that cannot be satisfied. `count-tolerance` is kept because it is the claim the oracle is making and it starts working the day an empty collection counts as evidence for a cardinality check.
- `test-review.contract.json` declares one behavior per planted registry row and each fragment-selection contract one behavior per oracle. `eval-quality`'s `designatedOracleIdOf` resolves AD-40's designated oracle only for a behavior declaring exactly one, and `score` votes a trial with the first oracle's state when none resolves, so a behavior grouping four plant oracles would have measured every probe against `O-001` whatever row it seeded and produced a defect catch rate of zero by construction. The demand is unchanged: the same oracles, all required, at the same severities, with all ten contracts still compiling and all 611 oracle checks still agreeing with their scorers. `trace.contract.json` still groups up to nine oracles under one behavior and is recorded as owing the same split.
- The eight fragment-selection contracts carry the prompt their witness legs actually send. Each leg's standard input was the sentence "The prompt the harness assembles for case X", which parses, compiles, and is scheduled by pre-flight, and then measures nothing when a real agent is handed it. `tools/generate-contracts.js` reads `buildPrompt` out of `test/eval-fragment-selection.js` now, the same way the trace witness already read its two prompts from its own harness. The contracts grew from around 20 kilobytes to around 90, which is what `trace.contract.json` already paid for the same correctness.
- `tea-test-review`'s request shape declares the environment names `vendorEnvironmentNames()` returns rather than three hand-written API keys. The adapter closes the child environment to `PATH` plus what the request declares, and `cli/test-review.js` resolves a stored login through `HOME`, so the authored list left a machine with a keychain login unable to run that contract's own pre-flight at all.
- All three eval harnesses probe the command they measure through `eval-quality`'s `createCommandLineAdapter`, replacing the `spawnSync` each of them owned. `eval-test-review.js` drives `tea-test-review` through the port and reads the verdict back as a tagged artifact, so a run that wrote no verdict is `absent` rather than an `existsSync` race followed by a `JSON.parse` in a `try`. `eval-fragment-selection.js` no longer calls `runAgent` in process: it runs `cli/fragment-selection-runner.js`, the command its eight contracts already name, so its live path and its contract describe one thing and the reply is parsed once, by the runner that produced it. `eval-trace.js` probes `tea-trace-runner` with the staged workspace as the authorization's working directory and reads both workflow artifacts off the observation's artifact map as tagged `json` and `text`, so an artifact the run never wrote is `absent` and a missing artifact, a summary that is not JSON is a parser failure, a thrown fault carries the port's class, and a non-zero exit maps through the runner's own table. Every run is authorized by the registry in `test/lib/probe-targets.js` before a process starts, capped at eight megabytes per stream, and bounded by two nested wall clocks where the inner one classifies and the outer one is a SIGKILL backstop a minute later. The scratch directory each fragment-selection run gets is now the authorization's own working directory, so the policy confines the run rather than a convention.
- `eval-quality` devDependency moved from `1.1.0` to `1.2.0`, for the repeatable-option spelling. The adapter builds argv from a key map, so `--env-pass A --env-pass B` had no spelling at all and the probe fixture had to pack a mode and a fragment list into one environment variable. An array value now emits the flag once per element, which is what lets the harnesses forward an operator's repeated `--agent-arg` and `--env-pass` unchanged.
- All three eval stub agents answer `--version`. Every harness pre-flight probes a runner that way before it will run anything, and without an answer the whole harness could not be driven end to end against a stub. All three suites now have a free, credential-free path through the real adapter, the real policy, and the real command, which is how the migration above was verified.

- The fragment-selection eval runs its agent in an empty scratch directory with a `read-only` declaration, and fails any run that leaves a file there or changes the repository; such a run is an environment failure and its reply is never scored. It ran the agent in the repository root with `Write` and `Edit` available while the manifest declared `read-only`. `tea-fragment-selection-runner` declares the same capability, because a selection is a reply and needs no file.
- The `test-review` eval passes `--isolate` on every review, so the agent writes only the report, the verdict, and the workflow's own temp files under sandbox-exec, bwrap, or the chmod fallback, whichever vendor is running. Isolation was on only under `CI`, so a laptop run handed the agent a writable checkout while the manifest declared `scoped-artifact-writes`. The pre-flight fails when no isolation backend exists, and a CLI exit 2 is classified as `environment-configuration`, because the agent never started and no verdict was ever going to exist.
- The `trace` eval declares `scoped-artifact-writes` to its runner and checks after every run that the repository did not change; a run that wrote into it is an environment failure and its artifacts are not read. Writes inside the staged workspace remain the run's deliverable, scored by `maxFixtureMutations` as before.
- The `test-review` scorer counts a finding against a file outside the review set as a definite false positive, carried as `outOfScope` beside the total, and matches paths on a `/` boundary; a bare suffix match had admitted `notcheckout.spec.ts` as `checkout.spec.ts`. `SCORER_VERSION` is 3. Every stored replay result grew `outOfScope: 0` and no other number moved; the derivations say so.
- `tools/validate-eval-schemas.js` runs each suite's declared preflight argv twice, once with a runner that does not exist and once with `node` standing in as a custom runner, and requires exit 2 with a recorded `environment-transport` class from the first and exit 0 with mode `preflight-only` from the second. It costs under a second and no credential.
- `eval-quality` devDependency moved from `1.0.0` to `1.1.0`. All nine contracts still compile.
- The `cli` job in `.github/workflows/quality.yaml` runs `test/test-test-review-cli.js` as a 3-way matrix (`TEA_CLI_TEST_SUITES`) instead of one serialized job. The file's 13 numbered suites spawn ~90 child processes between them, and one suite (CLI end-to-end) alone accounts for ~59 of those, so it now runs alone while the rest split into a fast unit shard and a real-git-fixture shard. `TEA_CLI_TEST_SUITES` unset (every local and pre-commit run) still runs all thirteen suites unchanged.
- `tea-test-review` now separates the uncapped `rawQualityScore` from the effective `qualityScore`. The effective score is capped by the highest finding severity (Critical 69, High 79, Medium 89, Low 99), controls the grade and score gate, and is accompanied by machine-readable score and verdict rules. Reports and PR comments show the raw score and the exact rule, so a Block can no longer appear beside 100/100 and a Critical finding can no longer receive grade A.
- Test-review human summaries now reserve Key Weaknesses for scored, registry-row-backed findings. Optional improvements and out-of-scope ideas use Advisory Observations instead, while empty and literal `n/a` list items are suppressed. Approved reports therefore no longer publish placeholder weaknesses.
- The `test-review` eval scores the verdict's own `findings` array. It re-parsed the markdown report with two regexes of its own, and the second parser disagreed with the CLI in both directions on fixtures this repository already keeps: it counted a Critical finding quoted inside a fenced example as real, which is the spoof `parse-report.js` strips fences to prevent, and it dropped a finding whose location line was unreadable and then refused to score the run at all. `findingsFromReport` is gone. Four stored replay cases moved with it and `SCORER_VERSION` is 2: two that scored unmeasurable now score a measured 0 of 9, and two now score real hits. A finding naming no file is counted as `unlocated` and kept out of the rate's denominator, because nothing can adjudicate it.
- The eval result record declares `contracts` as a list of `{path, version}` and its `schemaVersion` is `1.1.0`. One suite can be expressed as more than one contract: fragment selection is one suite across eight workflows.
- `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` declares `contracts` per suite and `trace`'s `caseCount` is 2. Each harness exports `caseIds()` and `tools/validate-eval-schemas.js` checks the declared count against it, so the behavioral check is no longer the manifest compared with itself. The same check fails when a generated contract is claimed by no suite.

- `e2e-trace-summary.json` declares `schema_version` 0.3.0. The version adds a `waivers` block, emitted only when a waiver register was found, and a `rejected_evidence` array that is always present and may be empty.
- The `test-review` precision metric is now `nonFalsePositiveRate` in code, console output, and the result schema. Its threshold is unchanged at 0.8. Only a finding against the clean fixture is a definite false positive; findings on seeded fixtures that match no planted row remain reported separately as `unattributed`, because they are unadjudicated rather than wrong.
- `zod` and `zod-to-json-schema` are declared devDependencies. `npm test` had depended on `zod` resolving transitively through Astro.
- The test-review report cross-check rejects a report that documents more Medium or Low findings than its `**Total Violations**:` line counted, which deducts less than its own findings require. Every finding in both findings sections is now bound to a real registry row, not just the Critical and High ones. The parser also accepts a backticked `**Row**` value and reads a finding's `**Location**:` line in the shapes live runs produce: a line range, a parenthesized line, or a bare path.
- `eval-quality` devDependency moved from `0.2.0` to `0.3.0`. All nine Behavioral Evaluation Contracts now compile: `0.3.0` closes the HTTP-only gap `test/contracts/README.md` recorded. The trace fragment-selection contract needed one more change to get there: `buildSelectionWitness` in `tools/generate-contracts.js` now authors an invariance witness, asserting two cases' selections agree rather than differ, for any workflow whose cases all mandate one fragment set. Trace's own eval deliberately keeps both cases' required sets identical, so it is the first to use it. `test/contracts/expected-status.json` and `test/contracts/README.md` carry the new baseline.
- `eval-quality` devDependency moved from `0.3.0` to `1.0.0`, published the same day (`0.4.0` never reached npm; `0.5.0` and `1.0.0` did). Compile status hasn't moved: all nine contracts still compile clean.
- The eight fragment-selection contracts describe a command that exists. Their request shape was transcribed against a runner nobody shipped and said the runner took `--model` and nothing else; `tools/generate-contracts.js` now reads `SELECTION_REQUEST_KEYS` and `DEFAULT_AGENT` off `cli/fragment-selection-runner.js`, so the shape moves when the command's option surface moves and `--check` fails until the contracts are regenerated. This is the rule the verdict response descriptor already follows for `tea-test-review`, and the reason is the one recorded there: the transcribed version had drifted by seven keys before anybody measured it. The shape gains `agent` as a required option and `HOME` and `USER` among the permitted environment names, because the adapter passes a child nothing but `PATH` and what the request declares, and both shipped vendors resolve a stored login through `HOME`. Each witness leg and each plan step binds `agent`.
- `parseSelection` moved from `test/eval-fragment-selection.js` to `cli/lib/parse-selection.js`, unchanged, and the harness re-exports it. The parser belongs to the command that produces the reply, and two copies would let a fenced spelling be accepted by the runner and rejected by the scorer.
- `cli/test-review.js` is mode 755 in the tree. It carries a `#!/usr/bin/env node` line and `package.json` declares it as a bin, and the probe adapter spawns the file itself, so the missing bit failed the spawn with `EACCES` before argv mattered.

### Fixed

- `docs/explanation/eval-quality-command-adapter.md` is reachable from the documentation site. It was the only page in `docs/explanation/` missing from the Starlight sidebar in `website/astro.config.mjs`, so it built and published and could be found only through the direct links in `README.md` and the roadmap. This is the same defect the roadmap page had before it was listed.

- Five stale counts in prose. `test/README.md` and `test/test-contracts.js` both said nine eval contracts where `test/contracts/` holds ten, and the `test/README.md` sentence naming the generator's sources omitted `fixtures/trace-eval/ground-truth.json`, which the tenth is generated from. `test/contracts/README.md` opened its operator-vocabulary section with "Two limits surfaced" above five of them. The `CHANGELOG.md` entry for the command-adapter document claimed four expressiveness limits where the document lists three. `test/evals/suite-manifest.json`'s own comment said the eight skills fragment selection spans are all still deferred, which stopped being true when `trace` gained a behavioral suite: six of the eight are deferred and `test-review` and `trace` are discharged by their own suites. `test/README.md`'s individual-suite list also gained `npm run test:probe-targets`, the check that drives every TEA command through the adapter.

- Every environment probe is bounded. The `--version` probe in all three eval harnesses, the macOS keychain lookup that decides whether `claude` has a stored login, and the three `git` questions about the working tree each ran as a `spawnSync` with no timeout. A pre-flight exists to fail before a paid matrix starts, and one that blocks forever shows CI a running job rather than a broken one. `test/lib/bounded-probe.js` gives all seven a ten-second deadline and a SIGKILL, and returns a reason so a caller can tell a timeout from a non-zero exit from an executable that is not on PATH. `npm run test:probe-targets` proves the bound against a child that ignores SIGTERM.

- A relative `--agent-cmd` on `eval:trace` passed the pre-flight and then lost every run. The pre-flight probes the executable from the harness's own directory, where the relative path resolves, and the runner executes in the staged workspace, where it does not, so `agent executable not found` arrived after the corpus had been validated and the workspaces staged. `parseArgs` resolves a path against the operator's directory now; a bare name stays a `PATH` lookup. Found by running the harness against the stub the way an operator would.
- The `trace` suite in `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` declared `"contracts": []` while every other suite named one, and nothing said the empty list was a gap rather than a decision. It names `test/contracts/trace.contract.json` now, and `test:probe-targets` fails a registered command no contract names in either direction.
- `tea-test-review --agent none` printed only the first 8 KB of a 15 KB prompt to a caller that captured it. `console.log` on a pipe is asynchronous and the `process.exit` that followed discarded whatever had not drained, so a Node parent received 8176 characters and nothing said so. The eval harness digests that output to detect a prompt change, which made every change past the 8 KB mark invisible to the digest, and the digest itself moved with the length of the temporary directory path because the cut fell in a different place. The command sets its exit code and returns, so the write finishes. A CLI test asserts the output reaches the prompt's last line.

- Eleven of the thirteen oracles in `test/contracts/test-review.contract.json` could never be evaluated. Every regex pattern the generator wrote was an optional group around a dot-star and a slash, `^(?:.*/)?name$`, and `eval-quality`'s regex operator refuses a quantifier nested inside a quantified group before matching anything, as a catastrophic-backtracking risk, reporting `budget-exhausted`. The compiler requires only the `^` and `$` anchors, so all nine contracts compiled clean for the whole time. The generator now writes the directory prefix as an alternation with an empty branch, `^(?:.*/|)name$`, which the operator accepts and which matches the same paths. Found by the first run of `npm run test:contract-oracles`; verified by evaluating O-001 over a stored verdict before and after.
- `runnerCapabilities` in `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` was enforced by nothing. The fragment-selection suite declared `read-only` while its harness ran the agent in the repository root with write tools, and the two `scoped-artifact-writes` suites had no check that their runner was scoped. The declaration is now the harness's own exported constant, applied through `cli/lib/run-agent.js`, and compared by `test:eval-schemas`; verified by flipping one suite's declaration and watching the check name the mismatch.
- `preflightArgs` for the fragment-selection and `trace` suites named `--validate-only`, which exits before the runner is probed, so `eval:all --preflight-only` reported both suites ready with no agent installed and a live matrix could start, spend money on the first suite, and only then discover the runner was missing. Both suites now declare `--preflight-only`, which probes the runner, and `test:eval-schemas` proves the probe by running the declared argv against a missing runner; verified by restoring `--validate-only` for one suite and watching the check fail with exit 0 where 2 was expected.
- Behavior `B-005` in the `test-review` contract, "coverage findings stay out of scope", was bound to an oracle no case could exercise and no scorer read. Its regex named a bare `.ts` or `.js` file with no dot in the stem, which matched neither `orders.service.ts` nor anything under `src/`, and the ground truth recorded the control as "not exercised". The oracle now states the review set itself: every located finding names one of the three files under review. The scorer applies the same rule, the new replay case trips both, and `test:contract-oracles` checks they agree on every stored verdict.
- `README.md` said `npm test` chains nineteen checks; it chained twenty when the probe-target check landed and chains twenty-one now. The count is derived from `package.json` by `npm run test:ci-coverage`.
- `readMatrix` in the `trace` eval harness closes a criterion section at any heading of the same depth or shallower. It closed one only at the next criterion-shaped heading, so `### Gap Analysis` left the last criterion's section open, and a test cited in the critical-gap prose beneath it was recorded as that criterion's evidence, where it resolved to nothing and lowered `evidenceCitationPrecision` for a correct run. Found while deriving the replay case for lines the parser must ignore: the hand derivation gave 10 citations and the code gave 11, with the AC-2 test attributed to AC-10. That case now pins the fix.
- The four top-level keys in `test/fixtures/trace-eval/ground-truth.json` that no check read. `nonDeterministicReportedValues` said the seeded set's `tests.cases` was 10 or 11 and its api test count 5 or 6; the evidence entries give 6 api tests under the first reading and 7 under the second, so the key's own arithmetic was wrong and nothing could have noticed, and step-04 has since made the count derivable by keeping a rejected test out of every total. It is gone, replaced by an `expectedTestInventory` block per set that `--validate-only` recomputes from the evidence entries and the harness scores as `tests.files`, `tests.cases` and `coverage.by_level.*.tests`, plus a check that the recommendations name every criterion in the critical, high and partial buckets, which is what the key's fourth entry asked for and nothing did. Its stale-versus-unverifiable entry was already scored through `expectedLiveEvidence.environmentDependentPair`. `negativeControls` and `rejectedCases` stay and are bound: each control id has a row in `NEGATIVE_CONTROL_ENFORCEMENT` naming the threshold that enforces it, each rejected case has a predicate in `REJECTED_CASE_EXCLUSIONS` that every fixture set is held to, and `--validate-only` fails on an id with no row, a row with no id, or a set that carries a rejected case; verified by adding each of those and watching the check fail. The fifth rejected case, a planted quality defect, has nothing in the corpus to check against and moved to the fixture README as prose. `oracles` described the harness, had nothing to check it against, and moved to the same README, rewritten to match the checks that exist.
- `test/test-probe-targets.js` uses `\u0000` where it had raw NUL bytes. Git read the whole file as binary and printed "Bin 0 -> 22164 bytes" where a reviewer needed a diff, which is the same defect `test/lib/eval-record.js` was fixed for. The escape is the same byte at runtime and every probe-target check still passes.
- `tools/build-docs.js` excludes the two maintainer handoff documents from `llms-full.txt`. The bundle had reached 599,996 characters of a 600,000 hard cap the file marks DO NOT CHANGE, leaving four characters, so the next documentation change of any size would have failed the build. Excluding the eval-quality roadmap and command-adapter design notes, which tell an agent consuming TEA nothing about doing testing work, returns the bundle to 571,309 characters. The cap counts characters and not bytes, which is why a byte measurement of the written file appeared to exceed it while the build passed.
- The trace clean fixture's AC-4 declared `FULL` coverage for a criterion its corpus does not establish. The text said the console list shows an expired token, and the only evidence is two API tests asserting what `GET /tenants/{tenant}/tokens` returns. Every other criterion in that set naming a rendered surface carries evidence at that level, AC-5 through a component test and AC-3 through an e2e test, so the declaration contradicted the corpus's own convention. Three live runs classified AC-4 as `PARTIAL` and `INTEGRATION-ONLY`, which was the correct reading, and the resulting gate mismatch false-positived the clean set to FAIL. The criterion now says the token listing reports the expired state, which is what the tests establish. Recording an inline classification rule in step-03 had not moved it, and the second measurement is what showed the corpus rather than the workflow was wrong.
- `test-review`'s verdict-stability gate is declared in `test/evals/suite-manifest.json` as `maxDistinctVerdicts`. The harness had always failed a run whose verdict moved between identical repetitions, and the manifest never mentioned it, which is the drift `npm run test:eval-schemas` exists to catch. Verified by deleting the new key and watching that check fail.
- `knownUnplanted` in the test-review ground truth changes the numbers instead of sitting there. A finding matching a row already adjudicated as a real unplanted defect is counted as `knownUnplantedHits` and kept out of `unattributed`, so that count means findings nobody has ruled on rather than findings a human already ruled on once per run. All twelve replay cases reproduce unchanged, so no scorer version moved.
- Prose across the repository asserted things the code contradicts, and an audit found fourteen of them. Corrected: `test/test-contracts.js` said every contract is blocked and that `eval-quality` is not a declared dependency, contradicting its own resolver comment forty lines down; `test/contracts/README.md` carried a present-tense heading saying the contracts do not compile; four separate files said no live eval run had ever been recorded, after all three suites were measured; the replay corpus holds twelve cases and two documents said eleven; the trace suite declares sixteen thresholds and the roadmap said thirteen; `docs/reference/execution-targets.md` said 41 of 59 knowledge fragments name Playwright or Cypress as 40, and asserted a seven-row portability split that was never re-derived across four revisions of the sentence; `README.md` called three eval harnesses two; and three comments in `tools/validate-criteria-fragments.js` reported registry coverage as 35/35 against 36 rows. Each replacement count was re-derived from the data rather than from the prose it replaces.
- The `test-review` eval harness defaulted to the `codex` runner while `eval-fragment-selection` and `eval-trace` defaulted to `claude`. All three default to `claude` now.
- `engines.node` is `>=22.0.0`, the current LTS and the one before it. It said `>=20.0.0`, which is older than any Node line still under long-term support.
- `redactArgs` drops a credential-shaped value anywhere in an argument. The pattern was start-anchored and was never applied to the value half of `--flag=value`, so `--extra=sk-live-abc`, `--header=ghp_...`, `Authorization: Bearer ghp_...` and `?token=ghp_...` all reached a result file CI uploads. A boundary in front of the prefix keeps `risk-based` and `task-runner` intact.
- `tools/generate-contracts.js` hashes through the one digest helper in `test/lib/eval-record.js`, which length-prefixes each file. Concatenating bytes let a byte moved from the tail of one step file to the head of the next leave the digest unchanged, and `bmad-testarch-ci` pins a multi-file list. Every `sourceSpecDigest` moved.
- Behavior B-003 grades `material`. Missing every planted HIGH defect graded `low`, below one out-of-scope finding. The grade is derived from the registry severity of the group's rows, on the rule that CRITICAL carries its own recall gate and everything else feeds the pooled one.
- A sensitivity-witness leg supplies every key its operation's request shape requires, read from the shape. The `test-review` legs restated the list and supplied only `files` against a shape requiring `files`, `json` and `agent`, so the contract parsed and then failed compilation under `undeclared-mandatory-input`.
- A finding's `**Location**:` keeps a path containing a space. `tests/checkout flow.spec.ts:38` published a finding against `tests/checkout` with no line: a real severity, a real contribution to the gate, and a file nobody can open. The Reviewed Files manifest already admitted spaced paths on the rule that a spaced token is a path when it ends in an extension, and the location parse now applies the same rule.
- `test/lib/eval-record.js` no longer contains a raw NUL byte. Git treated the whole file as binary and printed "Binary files differ" where a reviewer needed a diff. The escape hashes to the same byte, so no recorded digest moved.
- The trace harness scores a fabricated or duplicated criterion section. `readMatrix` discarded an unknown criterion id before it could be counted, so a report carrying every correct section plus an invented one and a contradictory second copy of another passed every threshold. Its stability signature also covered a fraction of what it scored, so citation resolution and live evidence could both flip between repetitions while the run reported itself stable.
- `eval:all`'s process exit is read off the summary it writes. A child that exited 0 while recording a measured quality failure produced a green process beside a result file declaring exit 1.
- `docs/reference/execution-targets.md` said the review-criteria registry carries 34 rows. It carries 36: `C1`-`C7`, `H1`-`H10`, `M1`-`M10`, `L1`-`L9`.
- The `test-design`, `atdd`, and `automate` step files gated Pact loading on "microservices indicators" and defined that phrase nowhere in the repository. `pactjs-utils-mandate.md` already carried the real definition, two or more independently deployable services in this repo that call each other, and the step files now state it at the point of use and say that a target-state architecture description naming a service count does not satisfy it on its own. Measured: the first live fragment-selection run loaded `contract-testing.md` into a system-level design off the phrase "describes three services", and the case scores clean after the change.
- The `framework` step file gated the playwright-utils mandate on `tea_use_playwright_utils` alone. The flag defaults to `true` on every stack, so a Python, Go, Java, or Ruby service with no `package.json` to install into still loaded `playwright-utils-mandate.md` and scaffolded sample files that project cannot run. Both the mandate branch and the disabled branch beneath it now test whether the project can carry the package at all, and `network-first.md` and `playwright-config.md` load only where a browser runs through Playwright.
- `trace` step-03 carries the coverage-classification rule inline instead of citing `checklist.md` by filename. The statuses are defined in `checklist.md` and nothing instructed the run to open it, so the classification was decided from the enum labels: the first two live runs classified a criterion that two API tests fully establish as `PARTIAL` and then `INTEGRATION-ONLY` against a truth of `FULL`, which false-positives the clean fixture's gate to FAIL. The rule that a criterion an appropriate single level establishes in full is `FULL`, and that `UNIT-ONLY` and `INTEGRATION-ONLY` mean a missing level left the criterion unestablished, is now stated where the decision is made.
- The trace eval harness reads a cited path that contains a space. `readMatrix` scanned free prose for a bare `path:line` pair, where the file extension is the only available boundary, so a spaced path was truncated to its last space-free segment and then failed to resolve against a recorded span, lowering `evidenceCitationPrecision` for a citation that was correct. Citations inside backticks are now read first and taken whole, which is the form `resources/traceability-matrix.example.md` already used; `trace-template.md` and step-03 now write and mandate that form, and step-03 says why. The undelimited prose scan stays as a fallback and delimited spans are cut out before it runs, so no citation is counted twice.
- `test/fixtures/test-review-eval/ground-truth.json` records the two real defects the fixtures carry that nothing plants, under `knownUnplanted`: `M4` on `orders.service.spec.ts`, which has four `it()` blocks and no `describe`, and `M1` on `checkout.spec.ts`, whose tests both navigate and then read data-dependent content with no intercept registered first. The first live run reported both as `unattributed` findings for a human to adjudicate, and this is that adjudication written down.
- `test/eval-test-review.js`'s `runReview` never passed `--output`, and `tea-test-review`'s default, `test-review.md` resolved against cwd, is the real repository root because the eval sets `cwd: PROJECT_ROOT`. The first live run of the `test-review` suite left its report sitting in the repo tree. The report is redirected into the same disposable `runDir` the JSON verdict already uses; only the verdict was ever scored.

- `test/contracts/test-review.contract.json`'s response descriptor is derived from the CLI instead of transcribed beside it. The transcription had drifted: `tea-test-review` can emit twenty-two verdict keys and `permittedKeys` listed fifteen, forbidding `reportedQualityScore`, `reportedRecommendation`, `unscorableTestArtifacts`, `gateFailures`, `waived`, `waiveReason` and `waiveUntil`. The first two are ordinary, because the CLI derives the score and the recommendation from the ledger and publishes the agent's own values beside them whenever the two disagree, so the descriptor rejected the shape most real reviews produce. `cli/lib/parse-report.js` now exports `PARSED_VERDICT_KEYS` and builds its verdict by projecting through it, `cli/test-review.js` composes that into an exported `VERDICT_KEYS` and asserts every published payload against it, and `tools/generate-contracts.js` reads `VERDICT_KEYS` for the descriptor, so `npm run test:contract-sources` fails the moment the CLI gains a verdict field. `requiredKeys` tightens from a hand-picked seven to all fourteen keys every verdict carries. The skip payload is declared as `SKIP_KEYS` and stays outside the descriptor.

- The `test-review` corpus planted a defect at `orders.service.spec.ts:39` under row `M3`, and `M3` is Multi-concern test, which does not cover a shape-only assertion. No registry row did. The registry's own rule reports a defect matching no row in prose with no severity and no deduction, and the harness matches on row identity, so a reviewer following the registry correctly could not produce that row and recall was capped at eight of nine for a reason that was not the reviewer's. Row `H10` now covers it and the ground truth names it.
- The `H4` plant was anchored on the declaration at `checkout.spec.ts:9` and the rule fires on the write at line 22, so a reviewer citing the line the rule names scored a miss.
- Each planted defect declares the set of lines a reviewer may cite for it, derived from the fixture, replacing the symmetric four-line radius. The radius was justified in one direction only, that citing the enclosing test still finds the defect, and it failed to reach the enclosing declaration for two plants while admitting lines 41 to 43 of a 40-line file for two others, which the review rubric's own fabricated-location penalty calls a defect. The pre-flight rejects an admitted line past the end of its file and a plant that does not admit its own line.

- `trace` reads, validates, and reports the waiver register at `{waiver_register_input}`. The checklist has validated waivers and the template has carried a waiver contract since they were written, and no step file told a run to load a register, so a run could follow every step and still ignore an unexpiring waiver signed by the engineer who wrote the code it waives. Step 5 section 2b now names each waiver's validity by the check ids in the checklist's Waiver Scenarios section, which stays the single definition of those rules. An invalid waiver is reported as invalid, a waived gap stays in the gap analysis, and Rule 6 is unchanged: the derived decision is the one Rules 1 to 5 produced.
- FULL is reachable for a criterion covered at one level. The checklist read "UNIT-ONLY - Only unit tests" and "INTEGRATION-ONLY - Only API/Component tests", which forced every single-level criterion out of FULL and contradicted the skill's own worked example, where two single-level criteria are FULL. The definition now turns on whether the evidence establishes the criterion, and names the level only where the missing level is what leaves it unestablished.
- `criticalGaps` matches the checklist's CRITICAL bucket. Step 4 built the list from `coverage === 'NONE'` while the checklist defined CRITICAL as P0 criteria without FULL coverage, so a P0 criterion at PARTIAL failed gate Rule 1 and appeared in no gap list. The report named a FAIL whose cause was missing from the section a reader goes to for it. The checklist is now the definition and step 4 builds from it.
- `tests.cases` is derivable. A test whose name claims a criterion its assertions do not establish stays out of that criterion's `tests` array, so `cases` and `by_level.*.tests` count evidence the trace accepted. Admitting it would have made the same counts mean "cases considered". Those tests are recorded in `rejected_evidence` and shown against their criterion in the report, so a reader sees which claims were read and turned down.
- `trace` emits a real value for `repo`. Step 5 wrote `repo: '{project_name}'` against a variable no `workflow.yaml` declared, so every summary and gate signal carried the literal placeholder. `project_name` is now declared from the TEA config the way `user_name` is, and an install whose config predates the key emits an empty string.
- Authentication, timeout, transport, parser, and missing-artifact failures are classified as environment failures and exit `2` in both live harnesses. `eval-test-review.js` previously discarded a timed-out or verdict-less run and scored the ones that survived, which reported a failed model call as a lower measured score.
- A run that completes fewer than its declared repetitions is unmeasurable and exits `2`. Variance and stability cannot be claimed from fewer observations than the gate declared.

- Documentation counts this branch had outrun. `npm test` chains nineteen checks where `README.md` said fourteen, the replay corpus holds eleven stored cases where the entry above said nine, the criteria registry maps 36 rows across 50 anchors where the README said 35 across 48, and one runner's `eval:all` makes 55 agent calls where the README said 51. Each count is now derived from the file that owns it.
